Privacy
The Supplier agrees:
to collect, use or disclose Personal Information obtained during the course of performing this Deed or any Contract only for the purposes of this Deed or the relevant Contract;
to maintain reasonable safeguards against loss, unauthorised access, use, modification or disclosure and other misuse of Personal Information held in connection with this Deed or any Contract;
not to commit any act, omission or engage in any practice which is contrary to the Privacy Act;
not to engage in any act or practice which if done or engaged in by an agency, would be a breach of an APP or a Registered APP Code;
not to engage in an act or practice that would breach an APP or a Registered APP Code (where applied to the Supplier);
to comply with any request under section 95C of the Privacy Act (relating to disclosure of any provisions of this Deed or a Contract that are inconsistent with an APP or a Registered APP Code binding on a party);
to notify the Customer Contract Manager immediately if the Supplier becomes aware of:
an eligible data breach; or
a breach or possible breach of any of the obligations contained in, or referred to in this clause 18.12 whether by the Supplier or its Personnel;
to notify the Customer Contract Manager promptly if the Supplier receives a complaint alleging an interference with the privacy of an individual by the Supplier or its Personnel;
to comply with any reasonable directions, guidelines, determinations or recommendations to the Supplier made by the Customer in respect of privacy issues and the management of Personal Information; and
to ensure that all Personnel required to deal with Personal Information for the purposes of this Deed or any Contract are made aware of the obligations of the Supplier set out in this clause 18.12.
Without limiting any of the Supplier's obligations under this clause 18.12, the Supplier must do all things necessary to assist the Customer to respond to an eligible data breach in accordance with the Privacy Act.
The Supplier's obligations in this Deed or any Contract are in addition to the Supplier's obligations (if any) under the Privacy Act and prevail to the extent of any lesser standard required under the Privacy Act.
Fraud
The Supplier must not, and must ensure that its Personnel do not, engage in any Fraud in relation to the performance of this Deed or any Contract.
The Supplier must immediately notify the Customer if the Supplier becomes aware of any actual or suspected Fraud in relation to the performance of this Deed or any Contract.
The Customer may conduct an investigation in relation to any actual or suspected Fraud in relation to the performance of this Deed or any Contract.  If the Customer conducts an investigation in accordance with this clause 18.13(c), the Supplier must provide all reasonable assistance and comply with any direction given by the Customer in relation to the investigation.
If an investigation conducted in accordance with clause 18.13(c) finds that:
the Supplier or any of its Personnel have committed Fraud; or
the Supplier failed to take reasonable steps to prevent Fraud by its Personnel;
then,
the Supplier must indemnify the Customer and pay on demand the amount of any Loss arising out of or in connection with the Fraud; and
the Customer may terminate this Deed or any Contract in accordance with clause 23.2.
The Supplier must advise its officers and employees that: 
they are Commonwealth public officials for the purposes of section 142.2 of the Criminal Code Act 1995 (Cth); 
acting with the intention of dishonestly obtaining a benefit for any entity is punishable by penalties including imprisonment; and
disclosures of "disclosable conduct" under the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) can be made directly to their supervisors within the Supplier, or to an Authorised Officer of the Customer as specified on the Customer's website (https://www.dcceew.gov.au/about/commitment/integrity), and where a disclosure of "disclosable conduct" is made to a supervisor within the Supplier, the supervisor is required under section 60A of the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) to pass information about the conduct to an Authorised Officer of the Customer. 
Criminal Code
The Supplier acknowledges that the giving of false or misleading information to the Commonwealth is a serious offence under section 137.1 of the Schedule to the Criminal Code Act 1995 (Cth).
The Supplier must ensure that any Subcontractor engaged in connection with this Deed or any Contract is aware of the information identified in this clause 18.14.
Public Interest Disclosure
Public officials (including service providers under a Commonwealth contract) who suspect wrongdoing within the Commonwealth public sector can raise their concerns under the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (PID Act).  Prior to making a disclosure, refer to information available at:https://www.ombudsman.gov.au/complaints/public-interest-disclosure-whistleblowing/information-for-disclosers.
All public interest disclosure matters (relating to this Deed or any Contract) should be referred to PID.Inbox@dcceew.gov.au or alternatively, the Integrity hotline on 1800 434 010. 
National Anti-Corruption Commission
The Supplier acknowledges that in providing the Goods and/or Services to the Customer under a Contract, it is a contracted service provider for the purposes of the National Anti-Corruption Commission Act 2022 (Cth) (NACC Act).
The Supplier must comply with any reasonable request, policy or direction issued by the Customer and otherwise cooperate with the Customer in relation to any action taken by the Customer required or authorised by the NACC Act.
Compliance with the Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct 
For the purposes of this clause, ‘Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct’ or ‘Code’ means the Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct, as published on 1 July 2024, as updated from time to time.
The Supplier must comply with, and ensure that it’s officers, employees, agents and subcontractors comply with, the Code in connection with the performance of this Contract.
The Supplier must: 
periodically monitor and assess its, and its officers’, employees’, and agents’ compliance with the Code; and 
on request from the Customer, promptly provide information regarding: 
the policies, frameworks, or systems it has established to monitor and assess compliance with the Code, and 
the Supplier’s compliance with clause 18.17(b). 
The Supplier must immediately issue the Customer a Notice on becoming aware of any breach of clause 18.17(b). The Notice must include a summary of the breach, the date that the breach occurred, and details of the personnel involved. 
Where the Customer identifies a possible breach of clause 18.17(b), it may issue the Supplier a Notice, and the Supplier must, within three (3) Business Days of receiving the Notice, either: 
where the Supplier considers a breach has not occurred: advise the Customer that there has not been a breach and provide information supporting that determination; or 
where the Supplier considers that a breach has occurred: issue a Notice under clause 18.17(d) and otherwise comply with its obligations under this clause 18.17. 
Notwithstanding clause 18.17(e), a Customer may notify the Supplier in writing that it considers that the Supplier has breached clause 18.17(b), in which case the Supplier must issue a Notice under clause 18.17(d) and otherwise comply with its obligations under this clause 18.17. 
A failure by the Supplier to comply with its obligations under any part of this clause will be a material breach of the Contract. 
Nothing in this clause or the Code limits, reduces, or derogates from the Supplier’s other obligations under the Contract. The Customer’s rights under this clause are in addition to and do not otherwise limit any other rights the Customer may have under the Contract. The performance by the Supplier of its obligations under this clause will be at no additional cost to the Customer. 
The Supplier agrees that the Customer or any other Commonwealth agency may take into account the Supplier’s compliance with the Code in any future approach to market or procurement process. 
Security
Compliance with Customer requirements and access to security classified information
The Supplier must, and must ensure that its Subcontractors and Personnel comply with: 
all relevant security and other requirements specified in the Australian Government Protective Security Policy Framework; 
any security requirements specified in item 14 of Schedule 1 (Deed Details);
any security requirements specified in an Official Order; and
any other security procedures or requirements notified, in writing, by the Customer to the Supplier.  The Supplier must comply with such a security procedure or requirement, from the date specified in the notice, or if none is specified, within 5 Business Days of receipt of the notice.
Where the Supplier and its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) may have access to security classified information of the Customer, the Supplier and its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) involved in providing the Goods and/or Services must complete, to the Customer's satisfaction, the Security Awareness Training Program, prior to accessing security classified information. 
The Supplier must notify the Customer of any changes to circumstances (including any changes in respect of its Personnel) which has or may affect the Supplier's capacity to supply the Goods and/or Services in accordance with the Customer's security requirements. 
If the Supplier, or its Personnel, breach any of the Customer's security requirements and this breach has a material adverse impact on the Customer, the Customer may terminate this Deed or any Contract in accordance with clause 23.2.
Security clearance
The Customer may, from time to time, notify the Supplier of the level of security or access clearance applicable to the Supplier's Personnel, and the date from which, or the period during which, that clearance will be effective and the Supplier must comply with and ensure its Personnel act in accordance with that notice. 
The Supplier must prevent access to security classified information by its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) who do not hold security clearances, or whose security clearances are revoked, lapse or are inadequate for the level of the security classified information, or who no longer require access to that information. 
The Supplier must report to the Customer any contact with security classified information by its Personnel (including Specified Personnel), where those Personnel do not hold security clearances, or hold security clearances that have been revoked, lapsed or are inadequate for the level of the security classified information, within 5 Business Days of the contact with the security classified information occurring.
The Supplier is responsible for all costs and expenses associated with obtaining security clearances. 
Storage of security classified information
This clause 19.3 applies where the Supplier will handle or store security classified information of the Customer outside the Customer's premises and/or systems.
The Supplier must ensure that the premises and facilities used to handle or store security classified information of the Customer meet the appropriate security level as determined by the Customer. 
The Supplier must have the appropriate systems able to meet designated information security standards for the electronic processing, storage, transmission and disposal of official information (being all that is information created, sent or received as part of the work of the Australian Government). 
The Supplier bears all the costs and expenses associated with ensuring that the storage of security classified information meets the appropriate security level as determined by the Customer. 
Security Incidents
The Supplier must report to the Customer any actual or suspected Security Incident within 5 Business Days of the actual or suspected Security Incident.
The Supplier must report any incidents or breaches of information and/or communications technology security, not involving Customer Material to CERT Australia within 5 Business Days of the incident or breach. 
Highest level of security classified information
The Supplier may have access to security classified information up to the level specified in item 13 of Schedule 1. 
Removal of Customer Data
The Supplier must not, and must ensure that its Personnel do not:
remove Customer Data or allow Customer Data to be removed from the Customer's premises; or 
take, process, transfer, manage or access Customer Data, or allow Customer Data to be taken, processed, transferred, managed or accessed outside of Australia,
without the Customer's prior written consent.
Unforeseen events
Occurrence of Unforeseen Events
A party (Affected Party) is excused from performing its obligations under this Deed or any Contract to the extent it is prevented by circumstances beyond its reasonable control, including acts of God, natural disasters, acts of war, riots, strikes outside that party's organisation and the improper acts and omissions of the other party over which they have no control (Unforeseen Events).
For the purposes of clause 20.1(a) an Unforeseen Event does not include (in the case of the Supplier's breach or non-performance of this Deed or any Contract) lack of funds for any reason, any strike specific to its Personnel, any Fraud or theft, any Personnel becoming unavailable for any reason including illness, injury or death or any disruption caused by any person exercising a right of access, inspection or audit.
Notice of Unforeseen Events
When an Unforeseen Event arises or is reasonably considered by the Affected Party to be an imminent possibility, the Affected Party must give notice of those circumstances to the other party as soon as possible, identifying the effect this will have on performance.
An Affected Party must make all reasonable efforts to minimise the effects of Unforeseen Events on the performance of this Deed and any Contract.
Termination 
If the Affected Party's performance of this Deed or the relevant Contract continues to be impacted by the Unforeseen Event for a period of more than [10] consecutive Business Days, the other party may terminate this Deed or the relevant Contract immediately by giving notice to the Affected Party.
If this Deed or any Contract is terminated under clause 20.3(a):
each party will bear its own costs and neither party will incur further liability to the other; and
where the Supplier is the Affected Party, it will be entitled to payment for work performed prior to the Unforeseen Event.
Dispute resolution
Procedure for dispute resolution
If a dispute arises under this Deed or any Contract, the parties must act in good faith and use reasonable endeavours to resolve the dispute as follows:
the party claiming a dispute has arisen must give the other party notice setting out the details of the dispute;
during the 10 Business Days after notice of the dispute is given, the Customer Contract Manager and Supplier Contract Manager must attempt to resolve the dispute (First Tier Response Process);
if the dispute cannot be resolved within 10 Business Days after notice of the dispute is given, the parties must, within a further 10 Business Days, use reasonable efforts through a meeting of an executive of each party (who must have authority to resolve the dispute) (or their nominees) to resolve the dispute;
if the dispute cannot be resolved within that further 10 Business Day period, the parties must refer the dispute to a mediator if one of them requests this;  
the parties:
may agree on an independent third person to act as the mediator; or
if they cannot agree on an independent third person within 5 Business Days after a request made under clause 21.1(d), the chairperson of the Resolution Institute or the chairperson's nominee will appoint a mediator; and
if the parties have been unable to resolve the dispute within a further 20 Business Days following the appointment of a mediator, either party may commence legal proceedings.
Costs
Each party will bear its own costs of complying with this clause 21. The parties must equally pay the costs of any mediator.
Confidentiality
Any information or documents disclosed by a party under this clause 21:
must be kept confidential; and
may only be used in an attempt to resolve the dispute.
Breach of this clause 
If a party breaches clauses 21.1, 21.2 or 21.3, the other party does not have to comply with that clause in relation to the dispute.
Termination of process
A party to a dispute may terminate the procedure for dispute resolution by giving notice to each other party after it has complied with clause 21.1.  Clauses 21.2 and 21.3 survive termination of the procedure for dispute resolution.
Obligations continue
Despite the existence of a dispute, the parties must continue to perform their obligations under this Deed or any Contract unless a direction is issued in accordance with clause 21.6(b).
If directed and notified in writing by the Customer to do so, the Supplier must cease performing the obligations of the Supplier under this Deed or any Contract which are specified in the Customer's notice until the Customer issues a further written notice to the Supplier directing it to resume performance of these obligations.
Exemption
This clause 21 does not apply to:
action under, or purportedly under, clause 22; or
legal proceedings commenced by either party seeking urgent interlocutory relief.
Suspension from Panel 
Suspension due to non-compliance 
The Customer may suspend the Supplier from providing Services under the Deed, by written notice to the Supplier, if: 
the Supplier has materially breached this Deed of Standing Offer (including a breach of a provision referenced in 23.2(a)(i)(A) or the Customer has a right to terminate this Deed; 
the Customer has received substantiated evidence of continuous or substantial negative feedback from one or more Commonwealth Agency in respect of the performance of the Supplier in connection with the Deed; or
the Customer reasonably considers that the Supplier is not providing the Services in accordance with this Standing Offer, 
Any suspension of the Supplier may apply to the Services in full, or any one or more Service categories or and Service subcategories and may be for any period of time. 
Before the Customer suspends the Supplier, the Customer will: 
provide the Supplier with the reasons for any proposed suspension; 
consider any feedback provided by the Supplier within the timeframes reasonably required by the Customer; and 
allow the Supplier a reasonable opportunity to rectify the issues that would entitle the Customer to suspend the Supplier, within the timeframes reasonably required by the Customer. 
If the Supplier Service Provider is suspended: 
the Supplier must not enter into any Contract in respect of the suspended Service categories or Service subcategories; 
the Supplier must promptly notify the Customer if it receives any request to enter into a Contract or any Request for Quotation in respect of the suspended Service categories or Service subcategories; 
must not respond to the request to enter into a Contract or Request for Quotation (other than to inform the requesting party that the Supplier is not able to respond to that request); and 
all other provisions of this Deed and any existing Contracts not affected by the suspension continue. 
The Customer may at any time lift a suspension by notifying the Supplier. The Customer must lift the suspension promptly after the Supplier demonstrates to the Customer’s reasonable satisfaction that the Supplier has rectified the issues that caused the suspension. 
If: 
any suspension is not lifted within three calendar months; 
The Customer has reasonable grounds to believe that the Supplier no longer supplies Services that meet the requirements for a Service category; or 
The Supplier requests, 
then the Customer may un-approve the Supplier in respect of any or all of the suspended or removed Services or Service categories or Service subcategories by written notification to the Supplier, and the Supplier agrees to promptly (and at its cost) enter into a variation to this Deed to give effect to this.
Termination
Termination by the Supplier for default
Without limiting any other rights or remedies the Supplier may have against the Customer arising out of or in connection with this Deed or any Contract, the Supplier may:
terminate this Deed effective immediately by giving notice to the Customer, if:
the Customer breaches a material provision of this Deed where that breach is not capable of remedy; or
the Customer breaches any provision of this Deed and fails to remedy the breach within 10 Business Days after receiving notice of the breach, or such longer period as may be specified in the notice of the breach; and
terminate any Contract effective immediately by giving notice to the Customer, if:
the Customer breaches a material provision of the Contract where that breach is not capable of remedy; or
the Customer breaches any provision of the Contract and fails to remedy the breach within 10 Business Days after receiving notice of the breach, or such longer period as may be specified in the notice of the breach.
Termination by the Customer for default
Without limiting any other rights or remedies the Customer may have against the Supplier arising out of or in connection with this Deed or any Contract, the Customer may:
terminate this Deed effective immediately by giving notice to the Supplier, if:
the Supplier breaches a material provision of this Deed where that breach is not capable of remedy; 
the Supplier breaches any provision of this Deed and fails to remedy the breach within 10 Business Days after receiving notice of the breach, or such longer period as may be specified in the notice of the breach; or
an event specified in clause 23.2(c) happens to the Supplier, except to the extent that the exercise of a right under this clause 23.2(a)(ii)(C) is prevented by law; and
terminate any Contract effective immediately by giving notice to the Supplier, if:
the Supplier breaches a material provision of the Contract where that breach is not capable of remedy;
the Supplier breaches any provision of the Contract and fails to remedy the breach within 10 Business Days after receiving notice of the breach, or such longer period as may be specified in the notice of the breach; or
an event specified in clause 23.2(c) happens to the Supplier, except to the extent that the exercise of a right under this clause 23.2(a)(ii)(C) is prevented by law.
Without limitation, for the purposes of clause 23.2(a)(i)(A) and clause 23.2(a)(ii)(A), each of the following constitutes a breach of a material provision:
the occurrence of an event or circumstance where this Deed or a Contract expressly states that the Customer may terminate this Deed or the relevant Contract, as applicable, on that specific event or circumstance occurring;
breach of a warranty under clause 4.7; and
a failure to comply with:
clause 12 (Intellectual Property);
clause 14 (Insurance);
clause 15.6 (Conflicts of interest);
clause 17 (Confidentiality); 
clause 18.7 (Work health and safety);
clause 18.8 (Child Safety);
clause 18.9 (Modern Slavery Act);
clause 18.12 (Privacy);
clause 19 (Security); or
clause 24.7 (Assignment and novation).
The Supplier must notify the Customer promptly if any of the following events or circumstances are experienced by the Supplier:
a Change of Control, where the prior written consent of the Customer has not been obtained; or
an Insolvency Event.
Potential Default
For the purposes of this clause Potential Default means any event, thing or circumstance which does not fall within the scope of clause 23.2 and which likely would, for a Contract:
result in the Supplier not being able to achieve a Milestone; or
give rise to a right of termination pursuant to clause 23.2 with the giving of notice or the passage of time.
The Supplier must notify the Customer immediately upon becoming aware of a Potential Default and must include the following information, substantially in the form of the template provided by the Customer (if any), in its notice:
the nature of and reason for the Potential Default;
how the Supplier proposes to rectify the Potential Default;
the date on which the Supplier proposes that the Potential Default will be rectified; and
any expected impact that the Potential Default may have on the delivery of the Goods and/or Services.
If the Customer becomes aware of a Potential Default either through the receipt of notice from the Supplier under clause 23.3(b) or by any other means, the Customer may provide the Supplier with a written notice setting out the nature of the Potential Default (Notice of Potential Default), any extension of time permitted and any requirements the Customer has in relation to the rectification of the Potential Default or reduction in scope of the relevant Contract.
On receipt of a Notice of Potential Default the Supplier must remedy the Potential Default or, where the Potential Default is not capable of being remedied, prepare a plan for the Customer's approval of the actions that the Supplier proposes to take to deal with the impact of the Potential Default (Potential Default Plan).
If:
the Supplier does nothing in response to the Notice of Potential Default; 
the Customer is not satisfied with the Potential Default Plan; or 
the Supplier subsequently fails to comply with the Potential Default Plan, 
the Customer acting reasonably, may by written notice to the Supplier, require the Supplier to immediately suspend the Goods and/or Services under the affected Contract (in whole or in part) and/or terminate the affected Contract in its entirety.
No action taken by the Customer pursuant to this clause 23.3 will:
relieve the Supplier from, or alter or affect, the Supplier's liabilities or responsibilities whether under this Deed or any Contract or otherwise according to law; or
prejudice the Customer's rights against the Supplier whether under this Deed or any Contract or otherwise according to law.
Termination by the Customer in relation to disrepute
The Supplier must promptly notify the Customer if it becomes aware of, or reasonably suspects, any act, omission, situation or occurrence has occurred, or may occur, which will bring about any of the circumstances described in clauses 23.4(b)(i) to 23.4(b)(iii).
Where the Supplier, its Personnel or any Related Body Corporate of the Supplier engages in any act, omission, situation or occurrence (either in connection with this Deed, any Contract or otherwise) that in the Customer's reasonable opinion:
brings the Supplier or the Customer into disrepute;
subjects the Supplier or the Customer to scandal, contempt, embarrassment or public ridicule; or
will, or will tend to, shock, insult, or offend the community, public morals or public decency,
the Customer may provide written notice of this to the Supplier and require the Supplier to provide, within 5 Business Days, reasons as to why this Deed or any Contract should not be terminated.
If the Supplier either:
fails to provide reasons in response to the notice issued under clause 23.4(b), or fails to provide these within the specific timeframe; or
the Customer is not reasonably satisfied with the reasons provided,
the Customer may terminate this Deed or any Contract by giving [10] Business Days' written notice to the Supplier.
In the event of termination under clause 23.4(c), the Customer will be liable only for payments due in relation to work performed before the date the termination takes effect.
The parties agree that the mere fact that the Supplier is performing the Goods and/or Services will be deemed not to bring about the circumstances described in clauses 23.4(b)(i) to 23.4(b)(iii).
Termination by the Customer for convenience
The Customer may, by notice to the Supplier, at any time and in its discretion:
terminate; or
reduce the scope of,
this Deed or any Contract for convenience.  Such termination or reduction in scope will take effect on and from the time specified in the notice.
Following receipt of a notice of termination or reduction of scope issued under clause 23.5(a), the Supplier must:
stop or reduce work in connection with any current Contracts affected by the notice;
comply with any other direction in the notice;
take all available steps to minimise Loss resulting from the termination or reduction; and
continue any work under any part of this Deed or any Contract, or under any other Contracts, not affected by the notice.
In the event of termination or reduction of scope under clause 23.5(a), the Customer will be liable only:
for payments due in relation to work performed before the date the termination or reduction takes effect; and
any reasonable costs incurred by the Supplier that are directly attributable to the termination or reduction, and then only when the Supplier substantiates these amounts to the satisfaction of the Customer Contract Manager.
None of the other provisions of this Deed or any Contract limit the Customer's ability to terminate or reduce the scope of this Deed or a Contract under this clause 23.5.
If the scope of a Contract is reduced, the Customer's liability to pay the Fees or to provide Customer Material abates in accordance with the reduction in the Contract.
Termination does not affect accrued rights
The expiry or termination of this Deed or any Contract does not affect any accrued rights or remedies of a party.
Termination does not affect Contracts
Termination of this Deed does not affect the continuance of any Contract formed under this Deed.
Termination of a Contract does not affect the continuance of any other Contract formed under this Deed.
General 
Nature of the relationship
The Supplier is engaged as an independent contractor.  The Supplier is not, by virtue of this Deed or any Contract, an officer, employer, partner or agent of the Customer.
The Supplier does not have any power or authority to bind or represent the Customer and agrees not to misrepresent its relationship with the Customer.
Counterparts
This Deed and any Contract may be executed in counterparts.  All executed counterparts constitute one document.
Further action
Each party must do, at its own expense, everything reasonably necessary (including executing documents) to give full effect to this Deed, any Contract and any transactions contemplated by this Deed or any Contract.
Severability
As far as possible all provisions of this Deed and any Contract will be interpreted so as not to be illegal, invalid or otherwise unenforceable.
If any part of this Deed or any Contract is or becomes illegal, invalid or unenforceable, the legality, validity or enforceability of the remainder of this Deed or the relevant Contract will not be affected and will be read as if that part had been severed.
Waiver
A failure or delay by a party to exercise any right or remedy it holds under this Deed, any Contract or at law, does not operate as a waiver of that right.
A single or partial exercise by a party of any right or remedy it holds under this Deed, any Contract or at law, does not prevent the party from exercising the right again or to the extent it has not fully exercised the right.
Variation
A variation of this Deed or any Contract is binding only if agreed in writing and signed by both parties.
Assignment and novation
The Supplier may only:
assign its rights; or
novate its rights and obligations,
under this Deed or any Contract with the prior written consent of the Customer.
Approvals and consents
Except where this Deed or any Contract expressly states otherwise, a party may, in its discretion, give conditionally or unconditionally or withhold any approval or consent under this Deed or any Contract.
Announcements
The Supplier must, before making a public announcement in connection with this Deed, any Contract or any transaction contemplated by it, obtain the Customer's agreement to the announcement, except if required by law or a regulatory body (including a relevant stock exchange).
If the Supplier is required by law or a regulatory body to make a public announcement in connection with this Deed, any Contract or any transaction contemplated by this Deed, the Supplier must, to the extent practicable, first consult with and take into account the reasonable requirements of the Customer.
Where reasonably practicable, the Customer must, on or before making a public announcement in connection with any Contract, or any transaction contemplated by it, provide notice to the Supplier of the general nature of the announcement.  For the avoidance of doubt, the Customer does not require the consent of the Supplier to the making of the announcement.
Disclosure of information
Notwithstanding any other provision of this Deed, the Customer may disclose information about this Deed, including Personal Information, required to be reported by the Customer.
Survival
Unless the contrary intention appears, the following provisions (and any rights arising on or from expiry or termination of this Deed or any Contract) survive expiry or termination of this Deed or any Contract:
clause 10.7 (Right of the Customer to recover money);
clause 11 (GST);
clause 12 (Intellectual Property);
clause 13 (Indemnities, risk and liability);
clause 14 (Insurance);
clause 15.4 (Subcontracting);
clause 16 (Recordkeeping and audit);
clause 17 (Confidentiality); 
clause 18.5 (Workplace Gender Equality);
clause 18.7 (Work health and safety);
clause 18.9 (Modern Slavery);
clause 18.12 (Privacy); 
clause 19 (Security);
clause 23.6 (Termination does not affect accrued rights); and
any other provision which expressly or by implication from its nature is intended to survive expiry or termination of this Deed or any Contract.
Applicable law
This Deed or any Contract, and any dispute arising out of or in connection with this Deed or any Contract, is to be governed in accordance with the laws of the Australian Capital Territory. 
Each party submits to the non-exclusive jurisdiction of the courts of the Australian Capital Territory.

Fees and Payment 
Prices for goods
The maximum price for each Good is set out below.  The parties may agree to lower prices for each Good in an Official Order.
Rates Card
The Rates Card at Table A below sets out the maximum daily rates that the Supplier is permitted to charge the Customer for the performance of the Services.  Lower daily rates may be agreed by the parties and specified in an Official Order.
Table A: Rates Card
The Supplier's daily rates must be based on an 8 hour Business Day.
For each Business Day, the maximum the Customer will pay for each of Personnel is the daily rate, regardless of whether the Personnel work for 8 or more hours in that Business Day.  That is, if the Personnel work for a period of 10 hours in any one Business Day, the Supplier will be paid as if the Personnel had worked 8 hours.
If Personnel work for only part of a Business Day, a pro rata calculation will be used.  The pro rata daily rate for a Personnel will be calculated on the Personnel's daily rate, divided by 8 hours and multiplied by the number of hours of Services performed by the Personnel in the Business Day.
Expenses
[Option 1]
No allowances or expenses are payable to the Supplier.
[Option 2]
Subject to item 2.1(b) below, the Customer will not pay any travel, accommodation or other expenses unless they have been pre-approved in writing by the Customer.
The Supplier will have travel and related accommodation arranged and paid for by the Customer in line with Customer processes. The Supplier will be reimbursed for meals and incidental expenses at non-SES rates.
[Option 3]
Subject to item 2.1(b) below, the Customer will not pay any travel, accommodation or other expenses unless they have been pre-approved in writing by the Customer.
The Supplier will be reimbursed for the actual cost of travel and related accommodation where they are pre-approved in writing by the Customer. The Supplier must submit an invoice for those expenses and the Customer will reimburse the Supplier in accordance with the invoicing procedures set out in this Schedule.

has been independently developed or acquired by the Confidant.

Permitted Purposes means:
use of the Confidential Information by the Confidant for the purpose of performing their duties in relation to the Deed of Standing Offer; and 
any other purpose that may be approved in writing by the Department from time to time.
Personnel means the Supplier's officers, employees, agents and Subcontractors, and the Subcontractor's officers, employees and agents. 
Subcontractor means a person that, under a subcontract, provides goods or services to, or performs work for, the Supplier (whether directly or indirectly) in order for the Supplier to meet its obligations under the Deed of Standing Offer.
Confidentiality Undertakings
The Confidant:
acknowledges and agrees that this Deed is for the benefit of the Department and is directly enforceable by the Department even though it is not a party to this Deed; 
must ensure that the Confidential Information is kept confidential and secure from disclosure;
must only use the Confidential Information for the Permitted Purposes; 
must not without the prior written consent of the Department, disclose or permit any person to disclose any of the Confidential Information to any person, other than as permitted under clause 2.2; and 
must promptly notify the Department of any unauthorised possession, disclosure or use of the Confidential Information contrary to this Deed, and take all steps necessary to prevent the recurrence of such possession, disclosure or use.
Clause 2.1 does not apply to a disclosure of any Confidential Information to the extent that the disclosure is for any of the following purposes:
to any other Personnel for a Permitted Purpose;
as required or authorised by law; and
as necessary for the conduct of any legal proceedings.
The Confidant acknowledges that it may be provided with the ability to access the Department-held information (in addition to the Confidential Information) in connection with its performance of the Permitted Purposes, including through access to the Department information technology systems.  Without limiting the Confidant's other obligations under this Deed or otherwise at law, the Confidant must not seek to access or use the Department-held information except to the extent strictly required to undertake the Permitted Purposes.
Return of Confidential Information
Without limiting the Confidant's obligations at law, the Confidant must deliver to the Department, or destroy or erase, as required by the Department, all documents (including electronically stored or otherwise) in its possession or control which contain or relate to the Confidential Information on the earlier of:
the date specified in a notice given by the Department (acting reasonably); and 
the time the documents and any other material are no longer required for the Permitted Purposes.
Variations
No term or provision of this Deed must be amended or varied unless such amendment or variation is agreed by the Department in writing.
Survival
This deed will survive the termination or expiry of any contract between the Supplier and the Confidant providing for the performance of services by the Confidant (whether directly or indirectly).
Applicable Law
The laws of the Australian Capital Territory must apply to this Deed.  The Confidant agrees to submit to the applicable jurisdiction of the courts of that Territory in respect of all matters arising out of this Deed.

Part 4a: Dishwashers (Determination 2015)
Notes: 
Drying Index test is conducted on the primary water connection mode with an unsoiled load. Energy consumption or water consumption measurements should not be verified during this test. The reference machine is not required.
PAEC test in this case is on the primary water connection mode with a soiled load, but the load does not have to be evaluated for wash performance. The reference machine is not required.
The water consumption test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
The combined test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load and using the reference machine. More than one test machine can be run in parallel with the reference machine.
Supplementary PAEC test is conducted on the supplementary water connection mode with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
Part 4b: Dishwashers (Determination 2024)
Notes: 
Drying Index test is conducted on the primary water connection mode with an unsoiled load. Energy consumption or water consumption measurements should not be verified during this test. The reference machine is not required.
PAEC test in this case is on the primary water connection mode with a soiled load, but the load does not have to be evaluated for wash performance. The reference machine is not required.
The water consumption test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
The combined test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load and using the reference machine. More than one test machine can be run in parallel with the reference machine.
Supplementary PAEC test is conducted on the supplementary water connection mode with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
Part 5a: Household Refrigerating Appliances (Determination 2012)
This Determination was superseded by Part 5b: Household Refrigerating Appliances (Determination 2019). Check testing is no longer carried out against this determination.
Part 5b: Household Refrigerating Appliances (Determination 2019)
Notes: 
During this test, the laboratory should make an assessment as to whether or not a circumvention device is present and if so if it was operational during the test.

Part 2b: Rotary Clothes Dryers (Determination 2024)

Part 3a: Clothes Washing Machines (Determination 2015)
Notes: 
Note, for combination washer dryers the quoted prices above should only relate to the cost of testing the washer function. Proposed prices associated with testing the dryer function should be reported in the separate “clothes dryer” table
Part 3b: Clothes Washing Machines (Determination 2024)
Notes: 
Note, for combination washer dryers the quoted prices above should only relate to the cost of testing the washer function. Proposed prices associated with testing the dryer function should be reported in the separate “clothes dryer” table
Part 4a: Dishwashers (Determination 2015)
Notes: 
Drying Index test is conducted on the primary water connection mode with an unsoiled load. Energy consumption or water consumption measurements should not be verified during this test. The reference machine is not required.
PAEC test in this case is on the primary water connection mode with a soiled load, but the load does not have to be evaluated for wash performance. The reference machine is not required.
The water consumption test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
The combined test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load and using the reference machine. More than one test machine can be run in parallel with the reference machine.
Supplementary PAEC test is conducted on the supplementary water connection mode with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
Part 4b: Dishwashers (Determination 2024)
Notes: 
Drying Index test is conducted on the primary water connection mode with an unsoiled load. Energy consumption or water consumption measurements should not be verified during this test. The reference machine is not required.
PAEC test in this case is on the primary water connection mode with a soiled load, but the load does not have to be evaluated for wash performance. The reference machine is not required.
The water consumption test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
The combined test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load and using the reference machine. More than one test machine can be run in parallel with the reference machine.
Supplementary PAEC test is conducted on the supplementary water connection mode with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
Part 5a: Household Refrigerating Appliances (Determination 2012)
This Determination was superseded by Part 5b: Household Refrigerating Appliances (Determination 2019). Check testing is no longer carried out against this determination.
Part 5b: Household Refrigerating Appliances (Determination 2019)
Notes: 
During this test, the laboratory should make an assessment as to whether or not a circumvention device is present and if so if it was operational during the test.

GEMS Product Item Requirements

Part 2a. Rotary Clothes Dryers (Determination 2015)
The following items shall be included in a laboratory test report commissioned for a GEMS check test: this is a check list and the format or order of items is not specified. Laboratories will be required to fill in a pro-forma check test report summarising the acquisition of the equipment and the test results, which is affixed to the front of the normal laboratory test report for each check test commissioned.
Testing and regulatory framework
Regulatory instrument: Greenhouse and Energy Minimum Standards (Rotary Clothes Dryers) Determination 2015
Test Standard applied (including any amendment): AS/NZS2442.1:1996 + A1 to A4
Regulatory Standard applied (including any amendment): AS/NZS2442.2:2000 + A1+A2
Any adopted variations to the standards: Determination Schedule 1 Variation to operation of AS/NZS2442.1, Determination Schedule 2 GEMS labelling requirements – GEMS star ratings and label requirements

Where possible, total dryer mass (including load) should be recorded on a balance throughout the test. Raw data should be made available for independent review on request.

Part 4a: Dishwashers (Determination 2015)
Notes: 
Drying Index test is conducted on the primary water connection mode with an unsoiled load. Energy consumption or water consumption measurements should not be verified during this test. The reference machine is not required.
PAEC test in this case is on the primary water connection mode with a soiled load, but the load does not have to be evaluated for wash performance. The reference machine is not required.
The water consumption test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
The combined test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load and using the reference machine. More than one test machine can be run in parallel with the reference machine.
Supplementary PAEC test is conducted on the supplementary water connection mode with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
Part 4b: Dishwashers (Determination 2024)
Notes: 
Drying Index test is conducted on the primary water connection mode with an unsoiled load. Energy consumption or water consumption measurements should not be verified during this test. The reference machine is not required.
PAEC test in this case is on the primary water connection mode with a soiled load, but the load does not have to be evaluated for wash performance. The reference machine is not required.
The water consumption test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.
The combined test is required to be undertaken with a soiled load and using the reference machine. More than one test machine can be run in parallel with the reference machine.
Supplementary PAEC test is conducted on the supplementary water connection mode with a soiled load. The reference machine is not required.

DOCUMENT ATTACHMENT: Approach to Market - GEMS Check Testing tender 2026.docx

APPROACH TO MARKET IN RELATION TO GREENHOUSE AND ENERGY MINIMUM STANDARDS (GEMS) CHECK TESTING SERVICES
Conditions of Approach to Market
ATM details
General conditions
Approach to market
The Commonwealth of Australia as represented by the Department of Climate Change, Energy, the Environment and Water ABN 63 573 932 849 (Customer) invites the submission of tenders for the provision of verification testing (check testing) of electrical and gas products registered under the Greenhouse and Energy Minimum Standards (GEMS) Act 2012, to ensure compliance with the performance and labelling requirements set out in GEMS determinations and in accordance with this Approach to Market (ATM).
This ATM comprises: 
these Conditions of Approach to Market;
‎Schedule 1 - Statement of Requirements; 
‎Schedule 2 - Tender Response Form; 
‎Schedule 3 - draft Deed;
‎Schedule 4 - Glossary;
‎Schedule 5 - Guidance on Statement of Tax Records;
In this ATM, unless the contrary intention appears, defined terms and acronyms have the same meaning given to them in ‎Schedule 4 or the draft Deed of Standing Offer.
The rules of interpretation as contained in the draft Deed of Standing Offer apply to this ATM.  If there is inconsistency between any part of this ATM, a document higher in the following list prevails over a document lower in the list to the extent of the inconsistency:
these Conditions of Approach to Market including documents incorporated by reference and ‎Schedule 4;
‎Schedule 5 - Guidance on Statement of Tax Records;
‎Schedule 2 - Tender Response Form; 
‎Schedule 1 - Statement of Requirements; and
‎Schedule 3 - draft Deed of Standing Offer.
Background
The Greenhouse and Energy Minimum Standards (GEMS) Act 2012 came into effect on 1 October 2012 and created a national framework for appliances and equipment energy efficiency in Australia. It aims to promote the development and adoption of appliances and equipment that use less energy and produce less greenhouse gases.
The GEMS Act is administered by the GEMS Regulator, who is based in the Australian Government Department of Climate Change, Energy, the Environment and Water. The GEMS Regulator, a position established by section 70 of the GEMS Act 2012, is responsible for monitoring and enforcing compliance with the GEMS Act.
Verification Testing (also known as Check Testing) refers to the activities undertaken by and on behalf of the GEMS Regulator, in forming a view as to whether a product (model) complies with the relevant GEMS determination to which it is registered, and the energy efficiency claims of the manufacturers and suppliers. 
A GEMS determination is a legislative instrument that specifies requirements for GEMS regulated products -including Minimum Energy Performance Standards (MEPS) and Energy Rating Labels (ERL). Currently there are 23 categorised products regulated under GEMS (refer attachment 16), all 23 are subject to MEPS and 8 carry ERL’s. Additional appliances continue to be considered and assessed for GEMS regulation. 
An effective independent verification testing scheme is an important element of the delivery of the Program. Verification testing is the process of determining whether a product performs according to claims made by suppliers and whether products meet the requirements set out in GEMS determinations.
The Department does not have the capability or expertise to conduct specialist laboratory testing on GEMS products in-house and therefore requires the services of external service providers. Accordingly, the Department invites suitably qualified suppliers to submit a tender for the check testing of GEMS products. 
The Department intends to establish a panel of one or more suppliers through a Deed of Standing Offer. The Deed of Standing Offer does not guarantee any volume of work. Services will be procured on an as-needed basis through the issue of Work Orders (or Orders) under the Deed. Each Work Order will form a separate contract and will specify the scope of services, deliverables, timeframes and any other relevant requirements for that engagement.
In accordance with the GEMS Check Testing Policy – with Validity criteria, laboratories selected for check testing must hold accreditation (for the specific GEMS product being tested) with the National Association of Testing Authorities (NATA) Australia: or another body that is signatory to either the Asia Pacific Accreditation Cooperation (APAC) Mutual Recognition Arrangement; or the International Laboratory Accreditation Cooperation (ILAC) Mutual Recognition Arrangement. The Greenhouse and Energy Minimum Standards (Authorisation Requirements for Testing GEMS Products) Instrument 2021, sets out those accreditation requirements. 
The procurement method to be used by the Department is an open tender process, where laboratories are invited to submit tenders to enter into a Deed of Standing Offer for the provision of check testing services on an as needs basis.
The anticipated timeframe for a contract is three (3) years and eight (8) months until 30 June 2030 with an optional extension of one (1) year.
The Customer's requirements are set out in more detail in:
‎Schedule 1 - Statement of Requirements; and
‎Schedule 3 - Draft Deed of Standing Offer.

Sustainable Procurement
The Australian Government has a commitment to sustainable procurement practices.  Sustainable procurement aims to reduce adverse social, environmental and economic impacts of purchased good and services throughout their life.  This includes considerations such as waste disposal and the cost of operations and maintenance of the life of the good and services.
Under the National Waste Policy Action Plan, the Commonwealth Government has committed to significantly increase the amount of recycled content it uses.  Buying goods made from recycled materials reduces the amount of waste resources sent to landfill, conserves Australia's precious natural resources and creates jobs.
Recognising this commitment, the Commonwealth Procurement Rules require the Customer to consider the Sustainable Procurement Guide, which is managed by the Customer, where there is opportunity for sustainability or use of recycled content.
The Customer's environmental sustainability requirements for this ATM, if any, are specified at section ‎2.5 of ‎Schedule 1 – Statement of Requirements.
Potential Suppliers are required to provide information about their environmental and sustainability practices and outcomes as specified at ‎Schedule 2 - ‎Attachment 6: Environmental Sustainability.

Statement of Requirements
Background
The GEMS Regulator adopts four approaches to its Compliance responsibilities under the GEMS Act: 
Educate: engage and support manufacturers and suppliers to better understand the requirements of the GEMS Act
Monitor: check testing, market surveillance, report on non-compliance
Investigate: assessment of non-compliance
Responding to non-compliance: suspending or cancellation of model’s registration, enforceable undertakings
The check testing process is the major quality assurance activity to test compliance under the GEMS Act. Achieving an effective check testing regime is a keyway of improving the impact of GEMS and the Equipment Energy Efficiency (E3) program. The threat of detection of non-compliance encourages compliance and helps establish a level playing field for all suppliers which encourages further investment and technological innovation.
Each year, the GEMS Regulator publishes a Compliance Monitoring program that sets out the primary focus to the monitoring compliance of GEMS regulated products for that year. Under the program, the GEMS Regulator tests GEMS registered products (check testing) to ensure they are meeting minimum energy performance standards (MEPS), Energy Rating Labelling (ERL) and energy efficiency and performance claims of manufacturers and suppliers. The GEMS Regulator may also choose any GEMS product for check testing that is not listed in the yearly program.
Currently there are 23 categorised products regulated under GEMS, all 23 are subject to MEPS and 8 carry ERLs. Attachment 16 outlines the GEMS products that are subject to this Approach to Market.
Check testing is an essential part of monitoring and enforcing the GEMS Act. The Check Testing Policy – with Validity Criteria outlines the principles used by the GEMS Regulator to verify, through laboratory testing, whether models of GEMS products meets the energy efficiency claims of manufacturers and suppliers and the relevant GEMS determination standards that apply to the product class which they are registered.  
Check Testing is normally conducted in two stages. The check testing process typically involves:
A Stage 1 check test, which is a full or part test to the relevant Standard, is performed usually on one sample of a product. This sample is generally purchased independently (usually through a retail outlet) and tested by a laboratory with the appropriate accreditation to test to the standard. 
the selected laboratory is responsible to ensure the integrity of the Stage 1 Check Testing results via controls in accordance with their accreditation relating to product identification, acquisition, transport, secure storage and access. This is to ensure that Stage 1 Check Testing is conducted in accordance with the relevant GEMS determination (including any applicable testing standards).
If the product passes, the registrant/supplier is notified that their model is compliant, and no further action is taken.
If the product fails, the registrant/supplier will be notified and provided with the check testing results. The supplier has the right of reply. In accordance with the check test policy, their response will underpin the GEMS Regulator’s decision to issue a notice under section 61 of the GEMS Act. The section 61 notice requires the supplier to either cancel the registration of the model or arrange for stage 2 check testing. Stage 2 check testing is usually organised and paid for by the supplier and requires up to three additional products of the same model to be check tested.
A product that has failed stage 1 check testing is securely stored by the test laboratory until the check test process is finalised to the approval and satisfaction of the GEMS Regulator. Upon finalisation the Department provides instructions to the laboratory for the disposal of the test product.
Goods and/or Services required
Description of Goods and/or Services
The Department requires the services of testing laboratories with suitable accreditation to conduct verification testing of products subject to regulation under the Greenhouse and Energy Minimum Standards (GEMS) Act 2012. Testing is required to verify that mandatory requirements are being met and that performance claims made by manufacturers and suppliers are accurate. Testing is carried out in accordance with GEMS determinations which outline an Australian or International test standard.
The successful tenderers are required to be accredited, or provide evidence that they are in the process of obtaining accreditation, to conduct the specific test or tests prescribed in the applicable GEMS determination by either:
the National Association of Testing Authorities, Australia (NATA); or
an accreditation body, other than NATA, that is a signatory to either: 
the Asia Pacific Accreditation Cooperation (APAC) Mutual Recognition Arrangement; or 
the International Laboratory Accreditation Cooperation (ILAC) Mutual Recognition Arrangement.
The successful tenderers must be authorised within the meaning of paragraph 2.1(b) above for any Order to be issued under the Deed of Standing Offer, or to deliver any Services under any existing Order.
Services requested include:
Purchase of test samples from retailers/ manufacturers (normally one sample but for some product types more than one sample may be required);
Conduct testing on product sample in accordance with the provisions of the relevant test standard and where applicable the requirements of your governing accreditation body (eg NATA) and prepare a test report. In general test reports should cover the following aspects:
•	Service Provider Details
•	Product Description
•	Test Standards and Variations
•	Instrumentation and set up
•	Product Specific Technical Content
•	Specific items that should be included are set out in Attachment 13.
In addition to producing the above test report, produce a “test summary report” on the provided proforma to be affixed to the front of the main report. This report shall be in the format set out at Attachment 15 (computer monitor used as example only) and would include the following 5 elements (in the order noted):
•	Document Control Sheet
•	Test Unit Management Record
•	Key Testing Details page
•	Results Summary Page 
•	Photos page (unless incorporated into the main report) including:
-	A general photo of the test unit and where relevant its packaging (showing all relevant faces)
-	A photo of the control panel/s if applicable
-	A photo of the energy label (if provided)
-	A photo of the product identification plate if applicable;
In addition to the reporting required at (ii) and (iii) above, Attachment 13 contains a list of items that are required to be included in check test reports.
Provide to the Department one (1) electronic copy of the test report in PDF format (not exceeding 3MB in size). Emails (including attachments and mail content itself) cannot exceed 20MB.
Securely store test samples prior to and following completion of testing (note storage of units following testing is estimated at between 2-12 weeks noting it can be longer).
On advice from the Department:
•	ready tested samples for ease of removal by a third party (ie re-pack into original packaging; place on a pallet etc); or
•	dispose of product by sale or destruction as directed by the Department and upon receipt of invoice from the Department, remit sale proceeds to the Department.
Provide an email update to the Department each month on the status of testing, i.e. products purchased, scheduled date for test, tests completed, products disposed of.
Mandatory requirements
The successful tenderers are required to be accredited, or provide evidence that they are in the process of obtaining accreditation, to conduct the specific test or tests prescribed in the applicable GEMS determination by either:
the National Association of Testing Authorities, Australia (NATA); or
an accreditation body, other than NATA, that is a signatory to either: 
the Asia Pacific Accreditation Cooperation (APAC) Mutual Recognition Arrangement; or 
the International Laboratory Accreditation Cooperation (ILAC) Mutual Recognition Arrangement.
Notwithstanding the use of 'must', 'shall', 'will' or other mandatory language, no other requirement in this Statement of Requirements or elsewhere in this ATM is to be treated as a 'mandatory requirement'.
Timeframes
It is expected that the Deed of Standing Offer will commence 1 November 2026 and continue for three (3) years and eight (8) months until 30 June 2030. There is also an extension option of one (1) year. In addition, the panel may be refreshed at any time to include additional product categories and/or suppliers.
Deliverables
The Supplier’s success in delivering the ordered Services will be measured by the following performance indicators: 
Procurement of products for check testing is carried out promptly and in accordance with the Customer’s instructions;
Testing is carried out on schedule (within 45 days of receiving the product in the laboratory unless otherwise agreed to) and in accordance with the relevant requirements; 
The results of the check testing are clearly articulated on the check test report template (proforma) and the template is fully completed in accordance with the accreditation requirements and the Customer’s instructions.
The test report is fully checked and any errors identified and corrected prior to issue to the Customer.
Provide a monthly email to the Customer showing the status for each model, i.e.:
Status of purchase of models (ordered/received in lab)
Test status (not commenced, commenced, completed, report issued). 
Upon request by customer, Supplier must provide results of their NATA assessment report/s and follow up submissions.
Environmental Sustainability
Potential environmental sustainability outcomes are:
All GEMS products that pass stage 1 check testing process are sent to a third-party contractor for auction where they are offered for sale to the public. 
GEMS products that fail stage 1 check testing are either; sent to a third-party contractor for destruction – (as required by law); or on request by the supplier, dispatched to the supplier for their research and development purposes only.

Guidance on Statement of Tax Records
Obtaining a Valid and Satisfactory STR, and other key requirements of the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy
Failure to fully satisfy the Shadow Economy Policy STR tender submission requirements including instructions received with an issued STR must result in the exclusion of your tender from the ATM process.
Read this guide thoroughly - it is prepared to provide support by summarising key policy and instruction requirements associated with the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy as well as facilitate a time efficient process for obtaining and submitting all STR Statements required for your organisation, ensuring your tender can progress to evaluation. 
This guide does not replace official instruction or guidance from the Australian Taxation Office or from The Treasury. 
In the event of conflicting guidance or inconsistency with this ATM and any guidance or communication from the Australian Taxation Office (ATO) or Treasury, please contact Gill Eborn at checktestingtender@dcceew.gov.au. 
What is a Statement of Tax Record (STR)?  How long is it valid?
A STR is an official document issued by the ATO that advises whether an applicant has a Satisfactory tax record and therefore if they have complied with their Australian Tax obligations.
An STR submitted with a tender must be valid at the ATM closing date.
An STR is valid for:
12 months from the time of issue – if you have an Australian tax record of four or more years.
6 months from the time of issue – if you have an Australian tax record of less than four years.
This ATM: Statement of Tax Record tender submission requirements
Please ensure you read detail of this requirement within this guide and review additional related conditions for participation requirements that may apply to you regarding the use of subcontractors 
Potential Suppliers must submit with their Tender either a: 
a Valid and Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record (STR) for the Potential Supplier; or
a receipt demonstrating that a STR has been requested from the ATO for the Potential Supplier and the Potential Supplier must provide a Valid and Satisfactory STR within 4 business days from the closing time of the ATM.
STRs that are forwarded within 4 business days after the closing date, following inclusion of an application receipt with the tender by closing date, must be emailed as directed in the ATM.
Where difficulties arise with the transmission of the document, Potential Suppliers should email checktestingtender@dcceew.gov.au.
The ABN on one of the STRs submitted (if more than one is required, refer steps below) must match the entity submitting the tender.
Where/How do I obtain a Statement of Tax Record?
An STR is available on request from the ATO and will be provided within four business days from application. 
The main avenues for application, are:
For business – ATO Business Portal
For sole traders – myGov account linked to the ATO
Further details, including information on alternative methods of submission, can be found at https://www.ato.gov.au/Business/Bus/Statement-of-tax-record/
Step 1: Determine if more than one STR is required 
Depending on your organisation structure you may need more than one STR to meet the minimum submission requirements of the tender. 
Table 1 below provides information about the STRs typically required for each organisational structure. 
To ensure you have supplied all required STRs in your tender, you should:
Review the table below for guidance; and
Comply with any additional requirements stated by the ATO on an STR issued to you, which will be apply specifically to your organisation. 
You can lodge your initial application for your organisation and the STR issued within 4 days will advise if further STRs are required, or;
You can save time within the tender deadline by applying for all likely required STRs simultaneously based on Table 1.
Table 1 – Required STR by organisational structure
Step 2 Review the requirements to obtain a Valid and Satisfactory STR
Criteria
A Valid and Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record will be issued if the 3 criteria below are met unless Section 6c of the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy (New business or Foreign entity ) applies –(see ‎4.4‎(b) below).
If you are confident the criteria listed below are met, or not sure, then begin the application as soon as possible.
An STR issued within 4 business days will then confirm whether your organisation does or does not meet the criteria.
If, however, you know you do not meet any of these criteria, then you have the opportunity and may have enough time to address the tasks necessary to meet the criteria.
Up-to-date registration requirements
The applicant is up-to-date with registration requirements which may include being registered for an Australian Business Number (ABN) and GST, and having a Tax File Number;
Lodgement of 90% of income tax, FBT returns and BAS statements due in the last 4 years.
The applicant has lodged at least 90 per cent of all income tax returns, Fringe Benefit Tax returns and Business Activity Statements that were due in the last four years or the period of operation if less than four years.  Reasonable delays in lodgements due to extensions agreed to by the Australian Taxation Office (ATO) will not affect the receipt of a satisfactory STR; and
Less than $10,00 in overdue debt due to the ATO
On the date the STR is issued, the applicant does not have $10,000 or greater in overdue debt due to the ATO (excluding debt subject to a taxation objection, review, or appeal under the provisions of Part IV C of the Taxation Administration Act 1953).  If the applicant has entered into a payment plan with the ATO, they will meet this criterion.
Requirements for new businesses and foreign Potential Suppliers 
(Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy Section 6(c)/ Part 8 - www.treasury.gov.au/publication/p2019-t369466)
Directors, partners, trustees or appropriate officers with the relevant authority of applicants that do not have an Australian tax record of at least four years (such as new businesses and foreign Potential Suppliers) will need to provide a statement to the ATO that states on behalf of the relevant Potential Supplier that it:
is a non-resident with no tax record or a tax record of less than four years in Australia (applicable to foreign entities);
will comply and pay all their Australian tax obligations (applicable to Australian and foreign entities);
has no tax related convictions in the last four years (applicable to Australian and foreign entities); and
is complying with its tax obligations outside of Australia (applicable to foreign Potential Suppliers and new Australian businesses).
Once the ATO receives the statements, the STR will be issued, stating either:
they do not have a tax record with the ATO but will be accepted as having a satisfactory tax record, or, 
if they have a tax record with the ATO of less than four years, they meet the criteria of having a satisfactory engagement with the Australian tax system but have a record of less than four years.
The STR will also be based on the tax records that are available to the ATO and be valid for six months only.
Step 3 Rectify any known non-compliant criteria
You should contact the ATO for any queries in addressing non-compliant criteria. 
Additional requirements of the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy – Potential Supplier obligations regarding subcontractor compliance.
Potential Suppliers must also:
hold copies of satisfactory and valid STRs for any first tier subcontractors that the Potential Supplier will engage to deliver goods or services with an estimated value of over $4 million including GST as part of the tender if known at the time of submission; and
ensure, if first tier subcontractors are subsequently engaged in respect of the contract, that the successful supplier or prime contractor holds satisfactory and valid STRs of their first-tier subcontractors where the subcontractors estimated contract value will be over $4 million including GST.
First tier subcontractors are subcontractors engaged by the business tendering or in direct contract with the Commonwealth entity.
IMPORTANT TIPS
Criteria - Consider the criteria early that is required to obtain a satisfactory STR and be prepared /allow time to correct/update any criteria your organisation may not initially satisfy. 
Apply early - Apply for an STR as early as possible from the Australian Taxation Office to allow enough time to address any issues that arise in the process (examples below). 
An STR issued as Valid and Satisfactory may also require additional STRs to be submitted to meet the minimum tender conditions - while an STR issued may indicate that your organisation has met the criteria for a Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record, the STR may also state that Potential Suppliers need to obtain further STRs, depending on their organisational structure (e.g. trust, partnership, tax consolidated group) etc.  This is clarified within this guidance and ultimately on STRs issued.  Below is an example:
Ensure a received STR is ‘Satisfactory’ - Potential Suppliers should ensure that an STR issued is actually a Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record and that the STR does not state that your organisation has not met the criteria for a Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record.
Ensure you submit the official STR and not supporting statements issued with the STR - You will receive a report with your STR titled: ”For your information: Statement of Tax Record – Taxpayer Report”. 
This report is for your information only and cannot be accepted as an STR submitted for the ATM.
The following is an extract of this Taxpayer report:
This report should detail the elements of the Procurement Connected Policy criteria you met or did not meet, or both, however the STR should be referred to for official confirmation of having a Valid and Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record.
Any other Financial Statements/Reports do not satisfy the STR requirement.
A satisfactory STR is based on limited criteria designed to measure compliance with the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy.  It is not evidence of financial viability.
Be aware in the case of circumstances only allowing for a receipt for STR application to be provided by the ATM closing time that:
You may not have enough time to address any issues or additional requirements on the STR issued (i.e. if it is not yet Satisfactory) in the following days to fully comply with the policy.
You should provide the official receipt sent by email; an on-screen receipt may not always provide adequate information to meet the policy requirements in this regard. 
The following Definitions apply to the Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy:
Why is my tender excluded from consideration if I do not fully comply with this requirement?
The Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy applies to this ATM.
This policy requires that, for applicable ATM processes, Commonwealth entities MUST ensure that only Potential Suppliers who submit a Valid and Satisfactory Statement of Tax Record from the Australian Taxation Office within the policy stated timeframes are progressed to be evaluated to provide goods or services under the ATM process.
The aim of this policy is to reduce Shadow economy behaviour by businesses involved in Government procurement contracts and so increase the integrity of Commonwealth Government procurement. 
This is done through ensuring businesses that provide goods and services to the Government participate with the Australian tax system by providing a satisfactory Statement of Tax Record (STR).
The supply chain is becoming more complex and competitive, and its integrity can easily be undermined by illegal supplier practices that are often unknown to the purchaser and creates an unfair advantage.
The Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy is available at www.treasury.gov.au/publication/p2019-t369466.
Related Links
ATO STR information and application instructions: https://www.ato.gov.au/Business/Bus/Statement-of-tax-record/?page=1#Requesting_an_STR  (reference above)
Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy: www.treasury.gov.au/publication/p2019-t369466 (referenced above)
Additional Links: Shadow Economy Procurement Connected Policy FAQ for Potential Suppliers: https://treasury.gov.au/review/black-economy-procurement-connected-policy/faq-applicants
