THE PARTIES AGREE AS FOLLOWS:
INTERPRETATION
Definitions
The meanings of the acronyms and defined terms used in this Deed and in any Contract are set out in Schedule 2 (Glossary). 
Rules of interpretation 
In this Deed and in any Contract, unless the contrary intention appears:
a word that suggests one gender includes the other genders;
a singular word includes the plural, and vice versa;
a reference to a person includes a body politic, body corporate or partnership; 
if a word or phrase is defined, any other grammatical form of that word or phrase has a corresponding meaning; 
headings are for convenience only and do not affect interpretation;
a reference to "dollars", "AUD" or "$" is a reference to Australian dollars;
the word 'includes' in any form is not a word of limitation;
the meaning of general words is not limited by specific examples;
if the last day of any period for the doing of an action falls on a day which is not a Business Day, the action must be done no later than the end of the next Business Day;
a reference to a clause, paragraph, schedule or annexure is to a clause or paragraph of, or schedule or annexure to, this Deed;
a reference to any legislation, or legislative provision, includes any statutory modification, substitution or re-enactment of that legislation or legislative provision;
a reference to a "party" is to a party to this Deed and a reference to a party to a document includes the party's executors, administrators, successors and permitted assignees and substitutes;
"and/or" means either or both;
a rule of construction does not apply to the disadvantage of a party because the party was responsible for the preparation of this Deed or any part of it, or a Contract or any part of it;
any agreement, representation, warranty or indemnity by two or more parties (including where two or more persons are included in the same defined term) binds them jointly and severally;
any agreement, representation, warranty or indemnity in favour of two or more parties (including where two or more persons are included in the same defined term) is for the benefit of them jointly and severally; and
the Schedules and any Attachments form part of this Deed.
Precedence of documents
If there is any inconsistency between the terms of this Deed, a descending order of precedence will be accorded to:
the agreed terms and conditions of this Deed and the Glossary; 
the other Schedules in their order of appearance; 
any Attachments to the Schedules; and 
documents incorporated by reference in this Deed,
so that the term in the higher ranked document will, to the extent of the inconsistency, prevail.
Entire agreement
To the extent permitted by law, this Deed and each Contract represents the entire agreement between the parties about its subject matter and replaces any previous understandings, agreements or representations.
Multi-agency Access
The Supplier agrees that any Commonwealth Agency and Commonwealth Company may order Goods and/or Services under this Deed. 
Commonwealth Agencies and Commonwealth Companies may request the supply of the Goods and/or Services under this Deed by making a request through Dynamic Sourcing For Panels (DS4P).
Term
Commencement
This Deed begins on the Commencement Date and (unless otherwise terminated earlier) continues for the duration of the Initial Deed Period (Term).
Option to extend
The Customer may extend the Term for the Option Period(s), on the terms and conditions then in effect, by giving the Supplier notice in writing at any time prior to the expiration of this Deed, unless otherwise specified at item 3 of Schedule 1 (Deed Details).
Any extensions exercised in accordance with clause 2.2(a) take effect from the end of the Initial Deed Period. 
Formation, scope and operation of the deed
Standing Offer 
This Deed constitutes a standing offer for the Term during which the Customer may, on an as-required basis, enter into a Contract with the Supplier in accordance with the process set out under clause 3.2. 
The range of Goods and/or Services which the Supplier offers to the Customer under this Deed are set out at Schedule 3 (Scope of Goods and/or Services). 
Ordering Goods and/or Services
If the Customer requires Goods and/or Services from the Supplier, the Customer Contract Manager may issue the Supplier with either an:
RFQ; or 
Official Order.
For the avoidance of doubt, the Customer may issue the Supplier with an Official Order pursuant to clause 3.2(a)(ii) without having issued an RFQ.
Upon the receipt of an RFQ, the Supplier must either:
provide a Quotation by the date the Quotation is required; or
decline the invitation.
A Quotation provided in accordance with clause 3.2(c)(i) must contain all the information required by the RFQ.
Quotations must remain open for acceptance by the Customer for a minimum of 30 days or for such time as specified by the Customer in the RFQ. 
Following the receipt of a Quotation, the Customer Contract Manager must within a reasonable period either: 
accept the Quotation by issuing an Official Order; or 
notify the Supplier that the Quotation has been rejected. 
If the Customer Contract Manager issues an Official Order to the Supplier, the Official Order will form the Contract for the relevant Goods and/or Services.
Terms of a Contract
Unless otherwise expressly agreed in the Official Order, the terms of each Contract will be those set out in the following documents in a descending order of precedence: 
the terms and conditions of this Deed as applicable to the Contract; 
the Attachments to this Deed as applicable to the Contract;
the Official Order; and 
any documents incorporated by express reference as part of the Contract,
so that the term in the higher ranked document will prevail to the extent of any inconsistency between the terms of these documents arises.
A Contract begins on the Order Commencement Date and continues for the Order Term, unless the Contract is terminated earlier in accordance with clause 22 of this Deed or otherwise in accordance with the terms of the relevant Contract. 
No Goods and/or Services must be provided by the Supplier, and no amount will become payable to the Supplier in respect of the Goods and/or Services, unless and until an Official Order has been issued by the Customer Contract Manager in accordance with clause 3.2(f)(i).
No Assurance of Orders
The Customer does not make any representations or in any way bind itself to place any specific number of orders, or any orders at all, during the Term.
Nothing in this Deed or any Contract is to be interpreted as providing that the: 
Supplier has the right to be the sole provider of the Goods and/or Services, or any part of the Goods and/or Services, to the Customer; or
Customer is prevented from seeking goods or services that are similar to, or the same as, the Goods and/or Services from other suppliers.
Deed review and refresh
The Customer reserves the right to, at any time during the Initial Deed Period and any extension period, review the operation of the Deed and do any one or more of the following:
Add or remove Service categories or Service subcategories (if specified) over the term of the Deed;
Suspend or remove the Supplier from the Deed for noncompliance with this Deed as further described in clause 22;
Approach the market to:
Add suppliers to the Deed in one or more Service category or Service subcategory; or
Add additional Service categories and Service subcategories to the Deed (for which the Supplier and other suppliers may tender to provide). 
Provision of Goods and/or Services
Supplier to provide Goods and/or Services
For each Contract formed in accordance with clause 3, the Supplier must deliver the Goods, and provide the relevant Services, with due care and skill and in accordance with this Deed and the relevant Contract.

Unless a different timeframe is specified in an Official Order, the Customer may accept or, where these do not comply with the requirements of this Deed or the relevant Contract, reject any Deliverable within 10 Business Days after the Deliverable is received.

require the Supplier to provide, at the Supplier's cost, replacement or rectified Deliverables which comply with the requirements of this Deed and the relevant Contract, within a period notified by the Customer; or

Title to the Deliverables passes to the Customer upon acceptance of these in accordance with clause 4.2(a).
Unsatisfactory performance
If:
the Supplier does not, in the reasonable opinion of the Customer, provide any part of the Goods and/or Services within the timeframes or to the standard required by the Customer; and
that failure is not remedied within the time period specified in an Official Order (and if no time period is specified, 10 Business Days) after the Supplier is given a notice requiring the unsatisfactory performance to be remedied,
the Customer may in its discretion:
direct the Supplier to remedy the failure, at the Supplier's expense, under the supervision of and in accordance with the directions of the Customer; 
require the Supplier to engage another person to provide the Goods and/or perform the Services within a reasonable timeframe, at the expense of the Supplier;
step-in and perform the Services itself, at the Supplier's expense; 
engage another person to provide the Goods and/or perform the Services at the Supplier's expense; or 
terminate this Deed or an affected Contract in accordance with clause 23.2.
For the avoidance of doubt, this clause 4.3 does not limit any other remedy that the Customer has in respect of unsatisfactory performance, including to recover any amounts calculated in accordance with any performance regime set out in the relevant Contract.
Risk to Goods

Hazardous Substances
This clause only applies if specified in item 17 of an Official Order.

a safety data sheet prepared in accordance with Safe Work Australia's 'Model Code of Practice: Preparation of Safety Data Sheets for Hazardous Chemicals’ (available at https://www.safeworkaustralia.gov.au/doc/model-code-practice-preparation-safety-data-sheets-hazardous-chemicals); or
an alternative form acceptable to the Customer,
as advised by the Customer; 
ensure that all documentation relating to any aspect of dealing with the Hazardous Substance clearly identifies the presence of the substance and (if applicable) the nature of the hazard; and
ensure that all affected Goods remain clearly labelled identifying the presence of the Hazardous Substance, nature of the hazard (if applicable) and appropriate safeguards.

within 5 calendar days of receipt of an eInvoice, where the Customer and the Supplier both have the capability to deliver and receive eInvoices through the Pan-European Public Procurement On-Line framework (Peppol); or

If the Customer Contract Manager rejects a claim under clause 10.3(a), the Customer Contract Manager must, within the time period specified in item 6 of Schedule 1 (Deed Details) (and if no time period is specified, 10 Business Days) of the receipt of the claim, notify the Supplier in writing of:
the need to resubmit the claim; 
the reasons for rejection of the claim; and
any action to be taken by the Supplier for the claim to be rendered correct for payment.
Late payment
If Customer fails to pay an approved claim:
within 5 calendar days of receipt of an eInvoice, where the Customer and Supplier both have the capability to deliver and receive eInvoices through Peppol; or 
in all other instances, within 20 calendar days after the receipt of the claim,
the Customer must pay interest on the unpaid amount at the General Interest Charge Rate calculated in respect of each day from the day after the amount was due and payable, up to and including the day that the Customer makes the late payment. 
Interest will only be payable in accordance with clause 10.4(a) if the interest amount owed by the Customer to the Supplier in respect of the late payment exceeds $100.00.
Withholding payment
If: 
the Supplier fails to provide a correctly rendered invoice in accordance with clause 10.6; or
the performance of the Supplier under this Deed or a Contract is in dispute,
the Customer may:
reject any related claim in accordance with clause 10.3(a); and
withhold all or part of any payment due to the Supplier under the relevant Contract, until the relevant issue is resolved.
Invoicing
An invoice is correctly rendered if it meets the requirements of a valid tax invoice under the GST Law and: 
bears a unique invoice number;
sets out the Supplier's name, ABN and the title of this Deed;
specifies the period covered by the invoice;
identifies the Official Order number and which part of the Goods and/or Services the invoice relates to;
specifies the total amount payable (including the amount of GST which is to be shown separately); 
is correctly addressed and calculated in accordance with this Deed and the relevant Contract; 
provides the date of issue for the tax invoice;
specifies the purchase order and/or contract reference number ([TechOne or SAP number]);
provides the Customer contact name; and
provides the Customer contact email address.
Right of the Customer to recover money
Without limiting the Customer's other rights or remedies under this Deed or any Contract, if:
the Customer elects in accordance with this Deed or any Contract to recover an amount from the Supplier; or 
the Supplier otherwise owes any debt to the Customer in relation to this Deed or any Contract, 
the Customer may do one or both of the following:
deduct the amount from any amounts payable by the Customer to the Supplier under this Deed or any Contract; or
give the Supplier a notice of the existence of a debt recoverable which must be paid by the Supplier within 30 days after receipt of the notice.
If any sum of money owed to the Customer is not received by its due date for payment, the Supplier must pay to the Customer interest at the General Interest Charge Rate calculated in respect of each day from the day after the amount was due and payable, up to and including the day that the Supplier makes the required payment.
Taxes
Unless expressly stated otherwise:
all amounts payable, consideration provided or monetary limits in this Deed or any Contract are inclusive of any taxes, duties or other government charges; and
all taxes, duties or other government charges levied in connection with this Deed or any Contract, are to be borne by the Supplier.
Payment Times Procurement Connected Policy Subcontracts
In this clause 10.9:
Correctly Rendered PT PCP Subcontractor Invoice means an invoice which is:
rendered in accordance with all of the requirements of the written contract (note that written contracts will often specify that the invoice must be a tax invoice and/or that certain information must be included in the invoice and/or the format of the invoice); and
for amounts that are correctly calculated and due for payment and payable under the terms of the written contract;
PT PCP means the Payment Times Procurement Connected Policy of the Commonwealth, as amended from time to time (available at https://treasury.gov.au/publication/p2021-183909);
PT PCP Policy Team means the relevant Minister, department or authority that administers or otherwise deals with the PT PCP on the relevant day;
PT PCP Evaluation Questionnaire means a questionnaire in substantially the form of Appendix C of the PT PCP;
PT PCP Purpose means:
the review, evaluation, monitoring, assessment and reporting on the PT PCP, including the compliance by those Customer's suppliers and their subcontractors that are Reporting Entities; or
improving payment times to PT PCP Subcontractors.
PT PCP Remediation Plan means a written remediation plan substantially in the form of Appendix D of the PT PCP;
PT PCP Subcontract means a subcontract between a Reporting Entity and another party (Other Party) where:
the subcontract is (wholly or in part) for the provision of goods or services for the purposes of this Deed or a Contract;
both parties are carrying on business in Australia; and
the component of the subcontract for the provision of goods or services for the purposes of this Deed or a Contract has a total value of less than (or is reasonably estimated will not exceed) $1,000,000 (GST inclusive) during the period of the subcontract, not including any options, extensions, renewals or other mechanisms that may be executed over the life of the subcontract (but including work/official orders entered into that are valued up to $1 million (GST inclusive) under standing offer (panel) arrangements), 
but does not include the following subcontracts:
subcontracts entered into prior to the Reporting Entities' tender response for this Deed;
subcontracts which contain standard terms and conditions put forward by the Other Party and which cannot reasonably be negotiated by the Reporting Entity; or
subcontracts for the purposes of:
procuring and consuming goods or services overseas; or
procuring real property, including leases and licences.
PT PCP Subcontractor means the party that is entitled to receive payment for the provision of goods or services under a PT PCP Subcontract;
Reporting Entity has the meaning given in the PTR Act;
Reporting Entity Subcontractor means any person that:
is a Reporting Entity; and
provides goods or services directly or indirectly to the Supplier for the purposes of this Deed or a Contract where the value of such goods or services is estimated to exceed $4,000,000 (GST inclusive).
'Reporting Entity Subcontract' has a corresponding meaning.
The Supplier must comply with the PT PCP.
If the Supplier enters into a PT PCP Subcontract in connection with a Contract, the Supplier must include in that subcontract:
a requirement for the Supplier to pay the PT PCP Subcontractor:
subject to clause 10.9(e), within 20 calendar days after the acknowledgement of the satisfactory delivery of the goods or services and receipt of a Correctly Rendered PT PCP Subcontractor Invoice.  If this period ends on a day that is not a Business Day, payment is due on the next Business Day; and 
subject to clause 10.9(f), for payments made by the Supplier after the payment is due, the unpaid amount plus interest on the unpaid amount calculated in accordance with clause 10.9(f);
a statement that the PT PCP applies to that subcontract; and 
a statement that the subcontractor may make a complaint to the PT PCP Policy Team or to the Customer in accordance with the PT PCP if there has been non-compliance with the requirements of this clause 10.9(c).
If the Supplier enters into a Reporting Entity Subcontract in anticipation of (or after) entering into a Contract, the Supplier must use reasonable endeavours to include in that subcontract: 
obligations equivalent to those in clause 10.9(c); and 
a requirement that if the Reporting Entity Subcontractor in turn enters into a Reporting Entity Subcontract, then that subcontract will include: 
obligations equivalent to those in clause 10.9(c); and 
obligations equivalent to this clause 10.9(d)(ii) (such that the obligations in this clause 10.9(d)(ii) are to continue to be flowed down the supply chain to all Reporting Entity Subcontractors). 
Clause 10.9(c)(i)(A) does not limit any obligation to comply with applicable legislation that provides for a shorter payment period than the period in clause 10.9(c)(i)(A). 
The Supplier is not required to pay interest in accordance with clause 10.9(c)(i)(B) if either: 
the Customer has failed to pay the Supplier in accordance with the timeframes and requirements under this Deed or a Contract; or 
the amount of the interest payable is less than $100 (inc GST). 
Interest payable under clause 10.9(c)(i)(B) will be simple interest at the General Interest Charge Rate calculated in respect of each day from the day after the amount was due and payable, up to and including the day that the Supplier makes the late payment.
If requested in writing by the Customer, the Supplier must properly complete and return a PT PCP Evaluation Questionnaire within 30 calendar days of the request.
If the Customer considers or becomes aware that the Supplier has not or may not have complied with: 
the requirements of clauses 10.9(b)—10.9(g); or 
the payment requirements of a PT PCP Subcontract, 
the Customer may direct the Supplier to provide to the Customer either or both of the following within the timeframes specified by the Customer: 
information to enable the Customer to review the Supplier's compliance; or 
a properly completed PT PCP Remediation Plan. 
The Supplier must complete all of the steps and activities contained in the PT PCP Remediation Plan provided under clause 10.9(i)(iv). 
If the Customer considers that the Supplier has failed to comply with any of its obligations under this clause 10.9, without limiting the Customer's rights and remedies at law or otherwise under this Deed or the relevant Contract, the Customer may do either or both of the following: 
take the failure or non-compliance into account as part of the Customer's monitoring of the Supplier's performance under this Deed or the relevant Contract; or 
report the non-compliance (and provide a copy of the completed PT PCP Remediation Plan) to the PT PCP Policy Team. 
The Supplier agrees that if it is the subject of a complaint in relation to its compliance with clauses 10.9(b)—10.9(g) or the associated payment provisions of a PT PCP Subcontract: 
it will not take any prejudicial action against the complainant due to the complaint or any investigation or inquiry in relation to the complaint; and 
it will cooperate in good faith with the Customer in connection with any investigation or inquiry and any attempt to resolve the complaint.
For any PT PCP Purpose, the Supplier consents to the Customer: 
using and sharing with any other Commonwealth Agency the information provided by the Supplier as part of a PT PCP Evaluation Questionnaire, a PT PCP Remediation Plan, or otherwise received or obtained by the Customer in connection with this Deed or a Contract or a PT PCP Subcontract; and 
receiving information obtained under, or in accordance with, the PTR Act (Protected Information) from an Entrusted Person and using such Protected Information. 
By submitting a PT PCP Evaluation Questionnaire or a PT PCP Remediation Plan or other document in connection with the PT PCP that includes any personal information within the meaning of Privacy Act, the Supplier warrants and represents that it has obtained all necessary consents in accordance with relevant privacy laws to the collection, use and disclosure of such information in the manner contemplated by clauses 10.9(m)–10.9(n).  The Supplier will provide evidence of such consents to the Customer on request.
A reference to the Customer in clauses 10.9(h), 10.9(i), 10.9(l)(ii) and 10.9(m)–10.9(n) includes the PT PCP Policy Team.
GST
Interpretation
In this clause 11, a word or expression defined in the GST Law has the meaning given to it in the GST Law.
GST gross up
If a party (Provider) makes a supply under or in connection with this Deed or a Contract in respect of which GST is payable, the recipient of the supply (Recipient) must pay to the Provider an additional amount equal to the GST payable on the supply (GST Amount).
Reimbursements
If a party must reimburse or indemnify another party for a Loss, the amount to be reimbursed or indemnified is first reduced by any input tax credit the other party is entitled to for the Loss and then increased in accordance with clause 11.2.
Exclusion of GST from calculations
If a payment is calculated by reference to, or as a specified percentage of, another amount or revenue stream, that payment will be calculated by reference to or as a specified percentage of the amount or revenue stream exclusive of GST.
Adjustments
If the GST payable by a Provider on any supply made under or in connection with this Deed or a Contract varies from the GST Amount paid or payable by the Recipient under clause 11.2 such that a further amount of GST is payable in relation to the supply or a refund or credit of GST is obtained in relation to the supply, then the Provider will provide a corresponding refund or credit to, or will be entitled to receive the amount of that variation from, the Recipient. 
Any payment, credit or refund under this clause is deemed to be a payment, credit or refund of the GST Amount payable under clause 11.2.
If an adjustment event occurs in relation to a supply, the Provider must issue an adjustment note to the Recipient in relation to that supply within 10 Business Days after becoming aware of the adjustment.
Tax invoice
A party need not make a payment for a taxable supply made under or in connection with this Deed or any Contract:
until it receives a tax invoice for the supply to which the payment relates; and 
in the case of any tax invoice issued by the Provider, unless the tax invoice complies with clause 10.6.
Intellectual Property
Foreground IP
Ownership of all Foreground IP vests on its creation in the Customer.
Unless otherwise specified in an Official Order, the Customer grants the Supplier a limited, royalty-free, revocable, world-wide and non-exclusive licence to use, reproduce, adapt, communicate and further sub-license (subject to the same restrictions (as a minimum) as the licence granted under this clause 12.1(b)) all Foreground IP solely for the purpose of performing its obligations under the relevant Contract.
Background IP and Third Party IP
Nothing in this Deed or a Contract affects the ownership of Background IP or Third Party IP.
The Supplier grants to the Customer a perpetual, irrevocable, royalty-free, fully-paid up, world-wide and non-exclusive licence to use, reproduce, adapt, communicate, exploit and further sub-license the Supplier's Background IP in order to receive the full benefit of the Goods and/or Services.
The Customer grants to the Supplier a licence to use the Customer's Background IP in accordance with clause 15.5(e).
The Supplier will use reasonable endeavours to ensure the Customer is granted a licence in respect of any Third Party IP on the best available commercial terms.
Intellectual Property warranty
The Supplier warrants and must ensure that:
the Intellectual Property rights assigned or licensed to the Customer under clause 12 are sufficient to enable the Customer to: 
use and maintain the Goods; and
receive the full benefit of the Services, 
contemplated under this Deed and any Contract; and 
an activity permitted, or purportedly permitted, by or under a licence or assignment of Intellectual Property to the Customer under this Deed or a Contract will not:
infringe the Intellectual Property rights or Moral Rights of any person; or 
give rise to any liability to make royalty or other payments to any person.  
The Supplier must notify the Customer if it becomes aware of any challenge, claim or proceeding arising in respect of any Intellectual Property assigned or licensed to the Customer under this Deed or any Contract or in respect of the Moral Rights of any person.
Moral Rights
The Supplier must use reasonable endeavours to obtain genuine consents in writing from its Personnel for the Supplier or the Customer to use material created by those Personnel in the manner anticipated under this Deed or a Contract, including consent to the Specified Acts.
Infringement claims
If a claim of infringement of Intellectual Property rights or Moral Rights is made or threatened by a third party, the Supplier must, at its expense, either:
obtain for Customer the right to continued use of the Deliverable; or
replace or modify the Deliverable so that the alleged infringement ceases, provided the Deliverable continues to provide the Customer with equivalent functionality and performance as specified in ‎Schedule 3 (Scope of Goods and/or Services) and/or in an Official Order. 
Indemnities, Risk and liability
Responsibility of Supplier
The Supplier is fully responsible for the performance of this Deed and any Contract and the Customer will not be responsible for any aspect of the delivery of the Goods and/or Services. 
Indemnity
The Supplier must indemnify the Customer (and any of the Customer's officers, employees or agents, for whom the Customer holds the benefit of this indemnity on trust) and pay on demand the amount of any Loss arising out of or in connection with any:
breach of this Deed or any Contract by the Supplier or its Personnel; 
negligent, unlawful or wilfully wrongful act or omission of the Supplier or its Personnel; 
use or disclosure by the Supplier or its Personnel of Personal Information or Confidential Information held or controlled in connection with this Deed or any Contract; or 
infringement or alleged infringement of a third party's Intellectual Property rights or Moral Rights where the infringement or alleged infringement arises out of any activity permitted or purportedly permitted under any assignment or licence referred to in clause 12 or otherwise under this Deed or any Contract.
The Supplier's liability under clause 13.2(a) will be reduced proportionately to the extent that the Supplier demonstrates the Loss arose out of or as a consequence of any negligent, unlawful or wilfully wrongful act or omission of the Customer or its officers, employees or agents. 
Insurance
Insurance policies
Subject to clause 14.2, in connection with this Deed and the performance of any Contract, the Supplier must have and maintain for the Term, valid and enforceable insurance policies for:
workers compensation; 
public liability;
products liability; 
professional indemnity; and
any other insurance policy specified at item 7 of Schedule 1 (Deed Details) or in an Official Order,
for the amounts and with the coverage specified at item 7 of Schedule 1 (Deed Details) or the Official Order. 
Insurance period
If the Supplier takes out a 'claims made' policy, which requires all claims and any fact situation or circumstance that might result in a claim to be notified within the period of insurance, the Supplier must maintain the policy (or a policy in like terms) during the Term and for a period of seven years on and from the expiry or the early termination of this Deed.
If the Supplier takes out an 'occurrence' policy, which requires the circumstances to which a claim relates to occur during the period of insurance whilst the notification of event can occur at any time subsequently, the Supplier must maintain the policy during the Term.
Evidence of insurance policies
The Supplier must provide the Customer with a copy of the insurance policies or certificates of currency on written request by the Customer.
Deed Management
Administration of the Deed
Each party must at all times during the Term ensure that it has nominated one suitably qualified administrator under this Deed.  As at the Commencement Date the administrator for each party will be: 
for the Customer, the Customer Contract Manager; and 
for the Supplier, the Supplier Contract Manager. 
The Supplier must:
liaise with the Customer Contract Manager as reasonably required; and
comply with reasonable directions of the Customer Contract Manager issued in accordance with this Deed or any Contract.
The giving of a direction by the Customer Contract Manager will not in any way reduce or otherwise affect the Supplier's obligations under this Deed or any Contract.
Notices
A notice, consent or other communication under this Deed or any Contract is only effective if it is in writing, and either:
signed by the person giving the notice and sent by mail; or
transmitted electronically or by email, by the person giving the notice.
A notice that complies with clause 15.2(a) is regarded as given and received: 
if sent by mail, upon delivery to the relevant address; or
if sent by email, on the day on which it is transmitted or, if transmitted after 5:00pm, on the next Business Day.
A party's address and contact details are those set out in Schedule 1 (Deed Details), or as the party notifies the sender from time to time.
Specified Personnel
The Supplier must ensure that:
performance of any Contract involves the use of the Specified Personnel; and
all Personnel are aware of and ensure compliance with the Supplier's obligations under this Deed and any relevant Contract. 
Where Specified Personnel are unable to undertake work in relation to a relevant Contract, the Supplier must: 
notify the Customer Contract Manager immediately; 
offer a suitable replacement acceptable to the Customer at the earliest opportunity; and
obtain the Customer's written consent prior to appointing any such replacement person.  The Customer must not unreasonably withhold consent.
The Customer may, by written notice to the Supplier, require the removal of any Personnel (including Specified Personnel) from the performance of a Contract.  The Supplier must promptly arrange for the removal of such Personnel and for a suitable replacement acceptable to the Customer at the earliest opportunity.
If the Supplier replaces any Personnel under this clause 15.3, it must do so at their own cost.
Subcontracting
The Supplier must not Subcontract the whole or any part of the work performed under this Deed or any Contract, except:
with the Customer's prior written approval;
to those entities set out in item 33 of the Official Order; and 
on such conditions (if any) the Customer considers appropriate. 
The Customer may revoke its consent to a Subcontractor, on reasonable grounds, at any time. 
The Supplier must ensure that: 
each Subcontractor agrees that the Customer may disclose the Subcontractor's name and the nature of the goods and/or services being subcontracted, publicly; and
every Subcontract approved contains clauses that impose obligations on the Subcontractor and grant rights to the Customer (either directly or through the Supplier) that are the same as those obligations imposed on the Supplier and those rights granted to the Customer under:
clause 12 (Intellectual Property);
clause 14 (Insurance);
clause 15.4 (Subcontracting);
clause 15.6 (Conflict of interest);
clause 16 (Recordkeeping and audit);
clause 17 (Confidentiality);
clause 18.5 (Workplace Gender Equality);
clause 18.7 (Work health and safety);
clause 18.8 (Child Safety);
clause 18.9 (Modern Slavery Act);
clause 18.10 (Freedom of Information);  
clause 18.12 (Privacy);
clause 19 (Security); and
clause 22 (Termination). 
None of the following reduce or limit the Supplier's obligations or liabilities under or in relation to this Deed or any Contract:
the Supplier Subcontracting any part of the work under this Deed or any Contract;
the Customer's approval of, or refusal to approve, a Subcontractor; or 
an act or omission of a Subcontractor. 
If requested by the Customer, the Supplier must promptly provide to the Customer a copy of any contract relating to: 
the Goods and/or Services; and/or 
any material relating to the engagement of the subcontractor.
Provision and use of Customer Material 
Except as expressly provided for in any Contract, the Customer is not required to provide any Customer Material to the Supplier to assist in the provision of the Goods and/or Services.
The Supplier must, and must ensure that its Personnel, only use the Customer Material:
for the purposes of the relevant Contract; 
in a manner consistent with any applicable requirements for the Customer Material, including any conditions set out in a Contract; and 
for a purpose for which the Customer Material was designed, manufactured or constructed.
The Supplier must:
protect all Customer Material from loss or damage; 
maintain Customer Material in good order; and
promptly return Customer Material to the Customer, upon request by the Customer. 
The Supplier will be liable for any loss of, or damage to, any Customer Material whilst it is in the possession or control of the Supplier, whether or not the loss or damage arises due to the fault of the Supplier.
To the extent that the Supplier needs to use any Customer Material to satisfy an express requirement of a Contract, the Customer grants to the Supplier a limited, royalty-free, revocable, world-wide and non-exclusive licence to use, reproduce, adapt, communicate and further sub-license (subject to the same restrictions (as a minimum) as the licence granted under this clause 15.5(e)) all Background IP in or related to the Customer Material, solely for the purpose of the Supplier performing its obligations under the relevant Contract.
The Supplier must not, without the prior written approval of the Customer Contract Manager:
modify Customer Material; or
transfer possession or control of Customer Material to any other person,
except to the extent reasonably necessary to enable the Supplier to satisfy an express requirement of a Contract. 
The Supplier acknowledges that Customer Material remains, at all times, the property of the Customer.
Conflict of interest
The Supplier warrants that as at the Commencement Date, to the best of its knowledge after making diligent inquiries, no conflict of interest exists or is likely to arise in relation to this Deed or any potential Contract, or any of its Personnel, that would impair its ability to impartially and objectively perform its obligations under this Deed or any Contract. 
If during the performance of any Contract a conflict of interest arises or is likely to arise, the Supplier must:
notify the Customer immediately in writing; 
provide full disclosure of all information relating to the conflict; 
detail how the Supplier intends to resolve or manage the conflict; and 
take such steps the Customer reasonably requires to resolve or otherwise deal with the conflict. 
Delays 
In the event of an actual or potential Delay, the Supplier must:
immediately notify the Customer of the nature and consequences of the Delay;
develop and provide to the Customer for approval a plan to work around or remove the problem in order to prevent, limit or rectify the Delay; 
regularly update the Customer in relation to the Delay as reasonably required by the Customer; and
take all steps reasonably required by the Customer to prevent, limit or rectify the Delay.
To the extent a Delay is caused by:
an Unforeseen Event, then the provisions of clause 20 apply; 
the Supplier (or its Personnel), any costs and expenses incurred by the Supplier to implement an approved workaround plan will be borne by the Supplier (this is without limitation to any other right or remedy of the Customer under this Deed or a Contract or otherwise); or 
the Customer's (or any third party under the Customer's control) failure to meet any of its obligations under this Deed or a Contract that are necessary for the Supplier to achieve a Milestone, then the relevant Milestone date will be extended by a period equal to the length of the Delay caused by the Customer.
Clause 15.7(b)(iii) will only apply where the Supplier takes all reasonable steps to mitigate the effects of the Delay caused by the Customer.
Progress meetings
The parties will meet at the times and in the manner set out in:
Schedule 4 (Management of the Deed) (or otherwise as agreed in writing by the parties) to discuss any issues in relation to this Deed or the provision of Goods and/or Services under any Contracts; and
any Official Order (or otherwise as agreed in writing between the parties) to discuss any issues in relation to the provision of the Goods and/or Services under the Contract.  
The Supplier must ensure that the Supplier Contract Manager and the Customer must ensure the Customer Contract Manager is reasonably available to attend such meetings and answer any queries relating to the provision of the Goods and/or Services raised by either party.
Reporting
The Supplier must provide the Customer with Reports in accordance with Schedule 4 (Management of the Deed) and any Official Order (or otherwise as agreed in writing by the parties).
Recordkeeping and audit
Records of work
The Supplier must keep full and accurate records relating to the performance of its obligations under this Deed and any Contract. 
The Supplier must maintain its records:
in a manner that enables them to be conveniently and properly audited; and 
for a period of at least 7 years from the date on which the records were created. 
Access, inspection and audit
The Supplier must:
following the provision of reasonable notice; and 
at reasonable times, 
permit the Customer, or any person nominated in writing by the Customer, to:
access the premises of the Supplier where a Contract is being performed; 
inspect and copy documentation, books and records, however stored, in the custody or control of the Supplier which are relevant to the performance of this Deed or any Contract; and
conduct audits relevant to the Supplier's performance under this Deed or any Contract.
The Supplier must provide all necessary facilities for the purpose of clause 16.2(a).
Without limiting clause 16.2(a), the Supplier acknowledges and agrees:
that the Auditor-General has the power to conduct audits of the Supplier and Subcontractors in relation to the performance of this Deed and any Contract; 
that the Auditor-General may give a copy of, or an extract from, a report on an audit in relation to this Deed or any Contract to any person who in the Auditor-General's opinion has a special interest in the report or the content of the extract; and 
to provide the Auditor-General the same access and inspection rights as those provided to the Customer under clause 16.2(a).
The Supplier must participate cooperatively in any audits conducted by the Customer, its nominee or the Auditor-General (as the case may be).  The participation in audits does not in any way reduce the Supplier's obligations under this Deed or any Contract. 
Information Commissioner Investigations
The Supplier acknowledges and agrees that it may be subject to investigation by the Information Commissioner under the Privacy Act. 
The Supplier acknowledges and agrees:
that it will participate cooperatively in any investigation by the Information Commissioner; and
to provide the Information Commissioner the same access and inspection rights as those provided to the Customer under clause 16.2(a).
The participation in an investigation conducted by the Information Commissioner does not in any way reduce the Supplier's obligations under this Deed or any Contract.
Assistance in conduct of Inquiries
The Supplier must provide the Customer with reasonable assistance in relation to any inquiry concerning the Goods, the Services, this Deed or any Contract.  For these purposes, an inquiry includes any administrative or statutory review, audit or inquiry (whether within or external to the Customer), any request for information directed to the Customer, and any inquiry conducted by Parliament or any Parliamentary committee.
Costs
Each party must bear its own costs of any reviews and/or audits relevant to the Supplier's performance under this Deed or any Contract.
Confidentiality
Use and disclosure of Confidential Information
Each party must not:
use any of the other party's Confidential Information except to the extent necessary to exercise its rights and perform its obligations under this Deed or any Contract; or 
disclose any of the other party's Confidential Information except in accordance with clause 17.2.
Permitted disclosures
The restriction in clause 17.1 does not apply to a disclosure of Confidential Information to the extent that the disclosure is:
required or authorised by law;
necessary for the conduct of any legal proceedings arising in connection with this Deed or any Contract;
made by the Customer, a Minister or Parliament: 
to another Commonwealth Agency, Minister or Parliament; or 
in accordance with statutory or portfolio duties or functions or for public accountability reasons, including following a request by Parliament, a Parliamentary committee or a Minister; or
to any of the following persons: 
a legal adviser, insurer, financier, auditor or accountant of a party to the extent required to enable them to perform those roles; or
any officers, employees or agents who need to know the information, in order to enable the person to exercise their rights or perform their duties under this Deed or any Contract.
Obligations on disclosure
Where a party discloses Confidential Information to another person pursuant to clause 17.2 (other than a disclosure by the Customer under clause 17.2(a)(iii)), the disclosing party must notify the receiving person that the information is Confidential Information and not provide the information unless the receiving person agrees to keep the information confidential.
If directed to do so by the Customer, the Supplier must ensure all Personnel execute a Deed of Confidentiality in the form set out at Schedule 9 (Deed of Confidentiality) prior to disclosing any Confidential Information in accordance with clause 17.2(a)(iv)(B).
Return or destruction of Confidential Information
Subject to clause 17.4(b), on termination or expiry of this Deed or the relevant Contract, the Supplier must immediately:
deliver to the Customer all documents and other materials containing, recording or referring to the Customer's Confidential Information; and 
erase or destroy all electronic records containing or referring to the Customer's Confidential Information,
which are in the possession, power or control of the Supplier or of any person to whom the Supplier has given access. 
The Supplier may retain any documents containing Confidential Information where such documents are:
retained in order to comply with any legal, professional or insurance obligations; or 
stored in electronic backups or records that are produced in the normal course, where it is not reasonably practicable to destroy such backups or records. 
Return or destruction of Confidential Information does not release the Supplier from its obligations under this clause 17.
Policy and law 
Compliance with laws and policies
The Supplier must, and must ensure that its Personnel, comply with:
all applicable laws of the Commonwealth, any State, Territory or local authority in providing the Goods and/or Services; and 
any applicable Commonwealth policies as set out in this Deed or a Contract or as notified by the Customer Contract Manager to the Supplier from time to time. 
Environmental sustainability
The Supplier must support the Customer's environmental sustainability policy objectives by, for each Contract: 
(laws) complying with all relevant environmental legislation applicable to the Supplier in the provision of the Goods and/or Services;
(Environmental Sustainability Plan) delivering to the Customer a draft Environmental Sustainability Plan that complies with clause 18.2(b) within [20] Business Days of the Order Commencement Date for Customer approval.  The Customer must within [10] Business Days notify the Supplier of its approval of the Supplier's draft Environmental Sustainability Plan or outline required changes for the draft Environmental Sustainability Plan. The Supplier must resubmit a revised draft Environmental Sustainability Plan which addresses the required changes within [10] Business Days of being notified of the required changes for approval and this clause will apply to any resubmitted draft Environmental Sustainability Plan;
(Environmental Sustainability Plan – reporting) reporting against the Supplier's approved Environmental Sustainability Plan(s) on an [annual] basis or when otherwise directed by the Customer to do so; 
(Environmental Management Practices) implementing and maintaining a documented Environmental Management System that: 
[Optional] address the requirements of ISO14001:2015 Environmental Management Systems; and
[Optional] is third party certified to ISO14001:2015 Environmental Management Systems;
(Continuous improvement – environmental sustainability) reviewing and reporting to the Customer on further opportunities to improve environmental sustainability outcomes and increase use of Recycled Content over the Order Term as opportunities arise and at regular intervals with a frequency of no less than [annually].  The Supplier must utilise these opportunities when directed by the Customer to do so, subject to the parties agreeing any reasonable amendments to the Contract Fees and payment arrangements directly associated with the relevant opportunities;
(Continuous improvement – waste management) reviewing and reporting to the Customer on further opportunities to improve its waste management performance through the Order Term as opportunities arise and at regular intervals with a frequency of no less than [annually].  The Supplier must utilise these opportunities when directed by the Customer to do so, subject to the parties agreeing any reasonable amendments to the Contract Fees and payment arrangements directly associated with the relevant opportunities; 
(Optimising appropriate use of Recycled Content) using Recycled Content where reasonably practicable in the Goods and/or Services and where that use will not compromise the safety, quality or capability of the Goods and/or Services; 
(Recycled Content standards and specifications) complying with all relevant Recycled Content standards and specifications associated with delivery of the Goods and/or Services, including the following:
[Insert key environmental sustainability and Recycled Content standards and specifications relevant to the procurement]; and
(Reporting use of Recycled Content) providing to the Customer a [monthly] report on the use of Recycled Content under the Contract, that includes at least the following information: 
a list of products containing Recycled Content and the mass and percentage of Recycled Content within each product; and
[Identify additional Recycled Content reporting requirements as required].  
The Supplier's Environmental Sustainability Plan must describe: 
[how the Supplier will work with other suppliers and the Customer to minimise or avoid waste to landfill throughout the life cycle of Goods delivered or goods consumed in delivery of the Services, including what happens to Goods at end of life;]
[how the Supplier proposes to use Recycled Content in the Goods and/or Services to the maximum extent possible without compromising quality, safety or capability;]
[a list of Recycled Content products proposed for use for the Goods and/or Services, including the proportion of Recycled Content used in each product and Recycled Content product supplier details;]
[how any Recycled Content proposed to be used under the Contract will be recorded, reported and verified;]
[how the Supplier will work with the Customer to continually improve the environmental sustainability of the Goods and/or Services over the Order Term;]
[any standards or specifications specific to use of Recycled Content in the Goods and/or Services;]
[any whole-of-life and/or life cycle costing tools proposed to be used under the Contract; and]
[any risks associated with the Environmental Sustainability Plan, and proposed risk management methodology.]
Indigenous Procurement Policy – Not used
Australian Industry Participation Plan – Not used
Workplace Gender Equality 
The Supplier must: 
comply with its obligations, if any, under the Workplace Gender Equality Act 2012 (Cth) (WGE Act); and
notify the Customer Contract Manager if it becomes non-compliant with the WGE Act during the Term of this Deed.
The Supplier must not enter into a Subcontract with a Subcontractor named by the Workplace Gender Equality Agency as an employer currently not complying with the WGE Act.
If the Deed Term exceeds 18 months, the Supplier must provide a current letter of compliance issued by the Workplace Gender Equality Agency within 18 months from the Commencement Date and following this, annually, to the Customer.
Shadow Economy Policy 
The Supplier must not enter into a Subcontract with a proposed Subcontractor (or agree to a novation of a Subcontract to a proposed Subcontractor) if the total value of all work under the Subcontract is expected to exceed $4 million (inc GST), unless the Supplier has obtained and holds one of the following Statements of Tax Record (STRs), as applicable to the proposed Subcontractor:
The Supplier must obtain and hold additional STRs in the following circumstances within 10 Business Days of the Supplier becoming aware of the circumstances arising:
The Supplier must provide the Customer with copies of the STRs referred to in clause 18.6(a) or 18.6(b) within 5 Business Days after receiving a written request from the Customer.
For the purposes of the Contract, an STR is taken to be:
satisfactory if the STR states that the entity has met the conditions, as set out in the Shadow Economy Policy, of having a satisfactory engagement with the Australian tax system; and
valid if the STR has not expired as at the date on which the STR is required to be provided or held.
Work health and safety
The Supplier must, and must ensure that its Personnel, comply with all applicable WHS Legislation and any relevant Customer policies and procedures relating to work health and safety.
The Supplier must:
ensure, so far as is reasonably practicable, the health and safety of its Personnel while those Personnel are at work in relation to this Deed or any Contract;
consult, co-operate and coordinate with the Customer in relation to its workplace health and safety duties;
on request, give all reasonable assistance to the Customer, by way of provision of information and documents, to assist the Customer and its officers to comply with their duties under the WHS Act; and
comply with any direction of the Customer relating to specified measures which the Customer considers are reasonably necessary for work health and safety.
If an event happens in relation to the Supplier's performance of a Contract that leads, or could lead, to the death, injury or illness of a person (Notifiable Incident), the Supplier must:
immediately notify the Customer of the Notifiable Incident and provide all relevant details;
as soon as possible after the Notifiable Incident:
formally investigate the Notifiable Incident to determine, so far as is possible, its cause and what adverse effects it will have on the Supplier's performance under the relevant Contract or any other Contracts, and health and safety; and
report to the Customer the outcomes of the formal investigation; and 
take all reasonable steps to:
remedy any effects of the Notifiable Incident on health and safety; and
ensure that an event or circumstance of the kind that caused the Notifiable Incident does not recur.
Child Safety
If any part of the Services involves the Supplier employing or engaging a person (whether as an officer, employee, contractor, or volunteer) that is required by State or Territory law to have a working with children check to undertake the Services or any part of the Services, the Supplier agrees:
to comply with all State, Territory or Commonwealth law relating to the employment or engagement of people who work or volunteer with children in relation to the Services, including mandatory reporting and working with children checks however described; and
if requested, provide the Customer at the Supplier's cost, an annual statement of compliance with this clause, in such form as may be specified by the Customer.
When child safety obligations may be relevant to a Subcontract, the Supplier must ensure that any subcontract entered into by the Supplier for the purposes of fulfilling the Supplier's obligations under this Deed or any Contract imposes on the Subcontractor the same obligations regarding child safety that the Supplier has under this Deed or any Contract.  Each subcontract must also require the same obligations (where relevant) to be included by the Subcontractor in any secondary subcontracts.
Modern Slavery Act
In this clause 18.9:
Grievance Mechanism means a process for handling a complaint or grievance about Modern Slavery practices that is consistent with the criteria set out in the Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights.
Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights means the United Nations' Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights: Implementing the United Nations "Protect, Respect and Remedy" Framework available at https://www.ohchr.org/documents/publications/guidingprinciplesbusinesshr_en.pdf.
Modern Slavery has the same meaning as it has in the Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth).
The Supplier must take reasonable steps to identify, assess and address risks of Modern Slavery practices in the operations and supply chains used in the provision of the Goods and/or Services.
The Supplier will ensure Personnel responsible for managing the operations and supply chains used in the performance of this Deed or any Contract have undertaken suitable training to be able to identify and report Modern Slavery.
Within one month of the Commencement Date, the Supplier will prepare and implement a Modern Slavery Risk Management Plan in relation to its performance of this Deed and any Contract and, if requested by the Customer, provide a copy of this plan to the Customer.  The Modern Slavery Risk Management Plan should at a minimum detail:
the Supplier's steps to identify and assess risks of Modern Slavery practices in the operations and supply chains used in the performance of this Deed and any Contract;
the Supplier's processes for addressing any Modern Slavery practices of which it becomes aware in the operations and supply chains used in the performance of this Deed and any Contract;
the content and timing of training for Personnel about Modern Slavery; and
the Grievance Mechanism/s available to Personnel.
The Supplier must comply with the Modern Slavery Risk Management Plan in its performance of this Deed and any Contract.  For the avoidance of doubt, nothing in this clause derogates from the Supplier's other obligations arising under any Contract or otherwise in relation to the provision of the Goods and/or Services.
If at any time the Supplier becomes aware of Modern Slavery practices in the operations and supply chains used in the performance of this Deed or any Contract, the Supplier must as soon as reasonably practicable:
take all reasonable action to address or remove these practices, including where relevant by addressing any practices of other entities in its supply chains; and
take all reasonable steps to remediate any adverse impacts caused or contributed to by the Supplier from these practices in accordance with the Guiding Principles on Business and Human Rights.
Without limiting clause 18.9(f), in performing this Deed and any Contract, the Supplier must:
not require Personnel to pay fees, charges, expenses or financial obligations incurred in order for the Personnel to secure their employment or placement (Recruitment Fees), regardless of the manner, timing or location of the imposition or collection of these Recruitment Fees;
not destroy or exclusively possess, whether permanently or otherwise, the travel or identity documents of Personnel; and
ensure Personnel can access a Grievance Mechanism to safely report any instances of Modern Slavery in the operations and supply chains used by the Supplier in its performance of this Deed or any Contract.
The Supplier must, within 10 Business Days of a written request from the Customer, provide, and must ensure that each of its Subcontractors provide, any information reasonably required by the Customer in relation to:
the Supplier;
a Subcontractor; and
the performance of this Deed or any Contract and relevant Subcontracts,
for the purposes of the Customer satisfying its obligations as a reporting entity under the Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth).
Freedom of Information
The Supplier acknowledges that:
this Deed and any Contract may be a 'Commonwealth contract' within the meaning of the Freedom of Information Act 1982 (Cth) (FOI Act); and
accordingly the Supplier may be a 'contracted service provider' within the meaning of the FOI Act.
If the Customer receives a request for a document under the FOI Act which:
was created by or is in the possession of the Supplier (or its Personnel); and
relates to, or was created in, the performance of this Deed or any Contract (other than the entry into this Deed or the relevant Contract),
then, if requested to do so by the Customer in writing, the Supplier must provide a copy of the document to the Customer at no additional cost to the Customer.
Archives Act
The Supplier must not transfer, or permit the transfer of, ownership or allow the destruction of a Commonwealth Record without the prior written approval of the Customer. 
All Commonwealth Records must be protected at all times from unauthorised access, use, misuse, damage or destruction by a third party.
Commonwealth Records must be delivered to the Customer after the expiry or termination of this Deed or the relevant Contract, as directed by the Customer.
Privacy
The Supplier agrees:
to collect, use or disclose Personal Information obtained during the course of performing this Deed or any Contract only for the purposes of this Deed or the relevant Contract;
to maintain reasonable safeguards against loss, unauthorised access, use, modification or disclosure and other misuse of Personal Information held in connection with this Deed or any Contract;
not to commit any act, omission or engage in any practice which is contrary to the Privacy Act;
not to engage in any act or practice which if done or engaged in by an agency, would be a breach of an APP or a Registered APP Code;
not to engage in an act or practice that would breach an APP or a Registered APP Code (where applied to the Supplier);
to comply with any request under section 95C of the Privacy Act (relating to disclosure of any provisions of this Deed or a Contract that are inconsistent with an APP or a Registered APP Code binding on a party);
to notify the Customer Contract Manager immediately if the Supplier becomes aware of:
an eligible data breach; or
a breach or possible breach of any of the obligations contained in, or referred to in this clause 18.12 whether by the Supplier or its Personnel;
to notify the Customer Contract Manager promptly if the Supplier receives a complaint alleging an interference with the privacy of an individual by the Supplier or its Personnel;
to comply with any reasonable directions, guidelines, determinations or recommendations to the Supplier made by the Customer in respect of privacy issues and the management of Personal Information; and
to ensure that all Personnel required to deal with Personal Information for the purposes of this Deed or any Contract are made aware of the obligations of the Supplier set out in this clause 18.12.
Without limiting any of the Supplier's obligations under this clause 18.12, the Supplier must do all things necessary to assist the Customer to respond to an eligible data breach in accordance with the Privacy Act.
The Supplier's obligations in this Deed or any Contract are in addition to the Supplier's obligations (if any) under the Privacy Act and prevail to the extent of any lesser standard required under the Privacy Act.
Fraud
The Supplier must not, and must ensure that its Personnel do not, engage in any Fraud in relation to the performance of this Deed or any Contract.
The Supplier must immediately notify the Customer if the Supplier becomes aware of any actual or suspected Fraud in relation to the performance of this Deed or any Contract.
The Customer may conduct an investigation in relation to any actual or suspected Fraud in relation to the performance of this Deed or any Contract.  If the Customer conducts an investigation in accordance with this clause 18.13(c), the Supplier must provide all reasonable assistance and comply with any direction given by the Customer in relation to the investigation.
If an investigation conducted in accordance with clause 18.13(c) finds that:
the Supplier or any of its Personnel have committed Fraud; or
the Supplier failed to take reasonable steps to prevent Fraud by its Personnel;
then,
the Supplier must indemnify the Customer and pay on demand the amount of any Loss arising out of or in connection with the Fraud; and
the Customer may terminate this Deed or any Contract in accordance with clause 23.2.
The Supplier must advise its officers and employees that: 
they are Commonwealth public officials for the purposes of section 142.2 of the Criminal Code Act 1995 (Cth); 
acting with the intention of dishonestly obtaining a benefit for any entity is punishable by penalties including imprisonment; and
disclosures of "disclosable conduct" under the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) can be made directly to their supervisors within the Supplier, or to an Authorised Officer of the Customer as specified on the Customer's website (https://www.dcceew.gov.au/about/commitment/integrity), and where a disclosure of "disclosable conduct" is made to a supervisor within the Supplier, the supervisor is required under section 60A of the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (Cth) to pass information about the conduct to an Authorised Officer of the Customer. 
Criminal Code
The Supplier acknowledges that the giving of false or misleading information to the Commonwealth is a serious offence under section 137.1 of the Schedule to the Criminal Code Act 1995 (Cth).
The Supplier must ensure that any Subcontractor engaged in connection with this Deed or any Contract is aware of the information identified in this clause 18.14.
Public Interest Disclosure
Public officials (including service providers under a Commonwealth contract) who suspect wrongdoing within the Commonwealth public sector can raise their concerns under the Public Interest Disclosure Act 2013 (PID Act).  Prior to making a disclosure, refer to information available at:https://www.ombudsman.gov.au/complaints/public-interest-disclosure-whistleblowing/information-for-disclosers.
All public interest disclosure matters (relating to this Deed or any Contract) should be referred to PID.Inbox@dcceew.gov.au or alternatively, the Integrity hotline on 1800 434 010. 
National Anti-Corruption Commission
The Supplier acknowledges that in providing the Goods and/or Services to the Customer under a Contract, it is a contracted service provider for the purposes of the National Anti-Corruption Commission Act 2022 (Cth) (NACC Act).
The Supplier must comply with any reasonable request, policy or direction issued by the Customer and otherwise cooperate with the Customer in relation to any action taken by the Customer required or authorised by the NACC Act.
Compliance with the Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct 
For the purposes of this clause, ‘Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct’ or ‘Code’ means the Commonwealth Supplier Code of Conduct, as published on 1 July 2024, as updated from time to time.
The Supplier must comply with, and ensure that it’s officers, employees, agents and subcontractors comply with, the Code in connection with the performance of this Contract.
The Supplier must: 
periodically monitor and assess its, and its officers’, employees’, and agents’ compliance with the Code; and 
on request from the Customer, promptly provide information regarding: 
the policies, frameworks, or systems it has established to monitor and assess compliance with the Code, and 
the Supplier’s compliance with clause 18.17(b). 
The Supplier must immediately issue the Customer a Notice on becoming aware of any breach of clause 18.17(b). The Notice must include a summary of the breach, the date that the breach occurred, and details of the personnel involved. 
Where the Customer identifies a possible breach of clause 18.17(b), it may issue the Supplier a Notice, and the Supplier must, within three (3) Business Days of receiving the Notice, either: 
where the Supplier considers a breach has not occurred: advise the Customer that there has not been a breach and provide information supporting that determination; or 
where the Supplier considers that a breach has occurred: issue a Notice under clause 18.17(d) and otherwise comply with its obligations under this clause 18.17. 
Notwithstanding clause 18.17(e), a Customer may notify the Supplier in writing that it considers that the Supplier has breached clause 18.17(b), in which case the Supplier must issue a Notice under clause 18.17(d) and otherwise comply with its obligations under this clause 18.17. 
A failure by the Supplier to comply with its obligations under any part of this clause will be a material breach of the Contract. 
Nothing in this clause or the Code limits, reduces, or derogates from the Supplier’s other obligations under the Contract. The Customer’s rights under this clause are in addition to and do not otherwise limit any other rights the Customer may have under the Contract. The performance by the Supplier of its obligations under this clause will be at no additional cost to the Customer. 
The Supplier agrees that the Customer or any other Commonwealth agency may take into account the Supplier’s compliance with the Code in any future approach to market or procurement process. 
Security
Compliance with Customer requirements and access to security classified information
The Supplier must, and must ensure that its Subcontractors and Personnel comply with: 
all relevant security and other requirements specified in the Australian Government Protective Security Policy Framework; 
any security requirements specified in item 14 of Schedule 1 (Deed Details);
any security requirements specified in an Official Order; and
any other security procedures or requirements notified, in writing, by the Customer to the Supplier.  The Supplier must comply with such a security procedure or requirement, from the date specified in the notice, or if none is specified, within 5 Business Days of receipt of the notice.
Where the Supplier and its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) may have access to security classified information of the Customer, the Supplier and its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) involved in providing the Goods and/or Services must complete, to the Customer's satisfaction, the Security Awareness Training Program, prior to accessing security classified information. 
The Supplier must notify the Customer of any changes to circumstances (including any changes in respect of its Personnel) which has or may affect the Supplier's capacity to supply the Goods and/or Services in accordance with the Customer's security requirements. 
If the Supplier, or its Personnel, breach any of the Customer's security requirements and this breach has a material adverse impact on the Customer, the Customer may terminate this Deed or any Contract in accordance with clause 23.2.
Security clearance
The Customer may, from time to time, notify the Supplier of the level of security or access clearance applicable to the Supplier's Personnel, and the date from which, or the period during which, that clearance will be effective and the Supplier must comply with and ensure its Personnel act in accordance with that notice. 
The Supplier must prevent access to security classified information by its Personnel (including Specified Personnel) who do not hold security clearances, or whose security clearances are revoked, lapse or are inadequate for the level of the security classified information, or who no longer require access to that information. 
The Supplier must report to the Customer any contact with security classified information by its Personnel (including Specified Personnel), where those Personnel do not hold security clearances, or hold security clearances that have been revoked, lapsed or are inadequate for the level of the security classified information, within 5 Business Days of the contact with the security classified information occurring.
The Supplier is responsible for all costs and expenses associated with obtaining security clearances. 
Storage of security classified information
This clause 19.3 applies where the Supplier will handle or store security classified information of the Customer outside the Customer's premises and/or systems.
The Supplier must ensure that the premises and facilities used to handle or store security classified information of the Customer meet the appropriate security level as determined by the Customer. 
The Supplier must have the appropriate systems able to meet designated information security standards for the electronic processing, storage, transmission and disposal of official information (being all that is information created, sent or received as part of the work of the Australian Government). 
The Supplier bears all the costs and expenses associated with ensuring that the storage of security classified information meets the appropriate security level as determined by the Customer. 
Security Incidents
The Supplier must report to the Customer any actual or suspected Security Incident within 5 Business Days of the actual or suspected Security Incident.
The Supplier must report any incidents or breaches of information and/or communications technology security, not involving Customer Material to CERT Australia within 5 Business Days of the incident or breach. 
Highest level of security classified information
The Supplier may have access to security classified information up to the level specified in item 13 of Schedule 1. 
Removal of Customer Data
The Supplier must not, and must ensure that its Personnel do not:
remove Customer Data or allow Customer Data to be removed from the Customer's premises; or 
take, process, transfer, manage or access Customer Data, or allow Customer Data to be taken, processed, transferred, managed or accessed outside of Australia,
without the Customer's prior written consent.
Unforeseen events
Occurrence of Unforeseen Events
A party (Affected Party) is excused from performing its obligations under this Deed or any Contract to the extent it is prevented by circumstances beyond its reasonable control, including acts of God, natural disasters, acts of war, riots, strikes outside that party's organisation and the improper acts and omissions of the other party over which they have no control (Unforeseen Events).
For the purposes of clause 20.1(a) an Unforeseen Event does not include (in the case of the Supplier's breach or non-performance of this Deed or any Contract) lack of funds for any reason, any strike specific to its Personnel, any Fraud or theft, any Personnel becoming unavailable for any reason including illness, injury or death or any disruption caused by any person exercising a right of access, inspection or audit.
Notice of Unforeseen Events
When an Unforeseen Event arises or is reasonably considered by the Affected Party to be an imminent possibility, the Affected Party must give notice of those circumstances to the other party as soon as possible, identifying the effect this will have on performance.
An Affected Party must make all reasonable efforts to minimise the effects of Unforeseen Events on the performance of this Deed and any Contract.
Termination 
If the Affected Party's performance of this Deed or the relevant Contract continues to be impacted by the Unforeseen Event for a period of more than [10] consecutive Business Days, the other party may terminate this Deed or the relevant Contract immediately by giving notice to the Affected Party.
If this Deed or any Contract is terminated under clause 20.3(a):
each party will bear its own costs and neither party will incur further liability to the other; and
where the Supplier is the Affected Party, it will be entitled to payment for work performed prior to the Unforeseen Event.
Dispute resolution
Procedure for dispute resolution
If a dispute arises under this Deed or any Contract, the parties must act in good faith and use reasonable endeavours to resolve the dispute as follows:
the party claiming a dispute has arisen must give the other party notice setting out the details of the dispute;
during the 10 Business Days after notice of the dispute is given, the Customer Contract Manager and Supplier Contract Manager must attempt to resolve the dispute (First Tier Response Process);
if the dispute cannot be resolved within 10 Business Days after notice of the dispute is given, the parties must, within a further 10 Business Days, use reasonable efforts through a meeting of an executive of each party (who must have authority to resolve the dispute) (or their nominees) to resolve the dispute;
if the dispute cannot be resolved within that further 10 Business Day period, the parties must refer the dispute to a mediator if one of them requests this;  
the parties:
may agree on an independent third person to act as the mediator; or
if they cannot agree on an independent third person within 5 Business Days after a request made under clause 21.1(d), the chairperson of the Resolution Institute or the chairperson's nominee will appoint a mediator; and
if the parties have been unable to resolve the dispute within a further 20 Business Days following the appointment of a mediator, either party may commence legal proceedings.
Costs
Each party will bear its own costs of complying with this clause 21. The parties must equally pay the costs of any mediator.
Confidentiality
Any information or documents disclosed by a party under this clause 21:
must be kept confidential; and
may only be used in an attempt to resolve the dispute.
Breach of this clause 
If a party breaches clauses 21.1, 21.2 or 21.3, the other party does not have to comply with that clause in relation to the dispute.
Termination of process
A party to a dispute may terminate the procedure for dispute resolution by giving notice to each other party after it has complied with clause 21.1.  Clauses 21.2 and 21.3 survive termination of the procedure for dispute resolution.
Obligations continue
Despite the existence of a dispute, the parties must continue to perform their obligations under this Deed or any Contract unless a direction is issued in accordance with clause 21.6(b).
If directed and notified in writing by the Customer to do so, the Supplier must cease performing the obligations of the Supplier under this Deed or any Contract which are specified in the Customer's notice until the Customer issues a further written notice to the Supplier directing it to resume performance of these obligations.
Exemption
This clause 21 does not apply to:
action under, or purportedly under, clause 22; or
legal proceedings commenced by either party seeking urgent interlocutory relief.
Suspension from Panel 
Suspension due to non-compliance 
The Customer may suspend the Supplier from providing Services under the Deed, by written notice to the Supplier, if: 
the Supplier has materially breached this Deed of Standing Offer (including a breach of a provision referenced in 23.2(a)(i)(A) or the Customer has a right to terminate this Deed; 
the Customer has received substantiated evidence of continuous or substantial negative feedback from one or more Commonwealth Agency in respect of the performance of the Supplier in connection with the Deed; or
the Customer reasonably considers that the Supplier is not providing the Services in accordance with this Standing Offer, 
Any suspension of the Supplier may apply to the Services in full, or any one or more Service categories or and Service subcategories and may be for any period of time. 
Before the Customer suspends the Supplier, the Customer will: 
provide the Supplier with the reasons for any proposed suspension; 
consider any feedback provided by the Supplier within the timeframes reasonably required by the Customer; and 
allow the Supplier a reasonable opportunity to rectify the issues that would entitle the Customer to suspend the Supplier, within the timeframes reasonably required by the Customer. 
If the Supplier Service Provider is suspended:
